# WinDesktopMgr backlog

The project's record of what is planned and what shipped. Every change that
merges to `main` updates this file in the same PR (see CLAUDE.md, "Backlog
Logging"): a planned item gets `✅ SHIPPED <date> (PR #NN)` in its Backlog
row; anything else gets a dated row in the Done table.

- **Public repo:** keep home-network details out. LAN IPs, MAC addresses,
  serials and service tags are written as `[redacted-ip]` / `[redacted-mac]` /
  `[redacted-tag]`, and security to-dos describe the action, not where a
  secret was found.
- **Automatic entries** (code-health scan findings, post-update regressions)
  are written by the tray to the gitignored `backlog_inbox.md`, never to this
  file, so the deployed checkout stays clean. Fold inbox rows in here with a
  normal PR.
- **Readable view:** `python scripts/backlog_html.py` writes `backlog.html`
  (gitignored), including any inbox rows.
- **History:** moved into the repo on 2026-11-22, rebuilt from the
  2026-10-26 `backlog.html` render after the original out-of-repo file was
  lost in a machine repair. Rows for PRs #234-#236 were added at that time.

### Backlog

| # | Feature | Value | Effort | Priority |
|---|---------|-------|--------|----------|
| 3 | **BIOS Settings Change Tracking** ✅ SHIPPED 2026-04-18 (commits 7595621 + follow-ups). New `bios_audit.py` module captures BIOS + security state (version, date, manufacturer, serial, board, Secure Boot, TPM, boot mode, VBS/HVCI/Cred Guard). Two-context capture: (a) tray polling loop every 15 min (user context — BIOS/board/VBS fields), (b) SystemHealthDiag scheduled task daily (elevated context — adds TPM/Secure Boot/Boot Mode). Each entry tagged with `context` field; diffs compare against previous snapshot of the **same** context to prevent null→value false positives when elevated context fills in what user context couldn't see. Append-only JSON history (`bios_audit_history.json`), dashboard info concern when changes detected in last 24h, always-visible "BIOS Audit Trail" panel on BIOS tab with context chips. |  |
| 4 | **Historical Trend Dashboard — Phase 1** ✅ SHIPPED 2026-04-20 (commit 4ef39fe, merge e833c6e). New `metrics_history.py` module: append-only JSON sampler hooked into `/api/dashboard/summary` as a best-effort side effect, throttled to one sample per 10 min, capped at 4000 entries (~27 days), atomic writes. New `GET /api/metrics/history` endpoint returns either all series (default 7-day window, 1-720h configurable) or a single metric. Dashboard gains a "Trends" card with inline-SVG sparklines for CPU %, memory %, max temp, concern counts, and per-drive disk % — no chart library dependency. Confirmed live after verify: 12 metric series recording (CPU/mem/concerns + 7 disk drives). 33 new unit tests + 9 new route tests, 84.7 % coverage. **Phase 2 (separate future item): trend insights / regression-slope detection / dedicated Trends tab / more metrics.** |  |
| 5 | **Smart Alerting with Thresholds** ✅ SHIPPED 2026-04-19 (commit 5c257e8). New `alerts.py` module with user-configurable thresholds for CPU / memory / disk / temperature. Default rules match the previous hardcoded thresholds. Persisted in `alert_rules.json` with atomic writes + bounds validation. Dashboard has a new "Alert Rules" card with inline threshold inputs + enable toggle + Save. Critical-overrides-warning so a value tripping both levels fires one concern. 26 new tests covering seeds, persistence, evaluator, routes, dashboard integration. |  |
| 6 | **PyInstaller Packaging + Auto-Update** | Bundle Python + dependencies into a single WinDesktopMgr.exe via PyInstaller. Double-click to run — no Python install needed. Includes GitHub Releases auto-update: app checks `api.github.com/repos/shigsdev/windesktopmgr/releases/latest` (public repo, no auth), downloads new exe if version is newer, swaps and restarts. Enables installing on wife's machine and any future machines. | Medium | P1 |
| 7 | **DNS Name Editing from UI — Path A (deep-link to router admin)** ✅ SHIPPED 2026-05-01 (commit a2ff8b0 → PR #13 → merge 0530fdb on origin/main). **Live verified post-merge on the tray running origin/main code**: `python dev.py verify` passed (89.4 s); new `POST /api/homenet/device/rescan-hostname` returns 400 on invalid MAC, 404 on unknown MAC, and 200 with `{"dns_hostname":"Kidde Basement","source":"orbi","ok":true}` for real Orbi-attached device `[redacted-mac]` (and the value persisted into `homenet_inventory.json`). **Re-scoped after spike**: Verizon CR1000A's `apply_abstract.cgi` writes need a fully reverse-engineered `diffCfg` payload buried in minified Vue.js code-split chunks (multi-hour brittle job). Orbi RBRE960 returns `ResponseCode 404 not implemented` on every Set/Edit SOAP action probed (`SetAttachedDeviceInfo`, `EditAttachDevice`, `SetDeviceName`, `EditDeviceList` × `DeviceInfo:1` / `DeviceConfig:1` / `LANConfigSecurity:1`). Strict spec (router-write-first, no local-only overrides) is unbuildable on this firmware combo without HTML scraping both admin UIs. Shipped instead: read-only `dns_hostname` field merged from router-authoritative scans (Verizon `cgi_basic.js` known_devices + Orbi `GetAttachDevice2`), preserved across ARP-only scans so a stale ARP refresh doesn't blank a router-sourced name. New `POST /api/homenet/device/rescan-hostname` endpoint pulls just one device's name without a full ARP+Wi-Fi cycle (~10s saved). Edit modal gains a read-only DNS Hostname row + 🔗 button (opens Verizon `/#/devices` for [redacted-subnet] devices, Orbi `/DEV_devices.htm` for 10.x devices) + 🔄 button (calls rescan endpoint, optimistic in-memory update of `_hnDevices` so the table reflects the new name without a tab close/reopen). Honors "no local-only overrides" because the local field is never editable directly — the router IS the source of truth. 12 new tests: TestHomeNetRescanHostname (7 — invalid MAC 400, unknown MAC 404, Verizon happy path with `_orbi_get_devices` assert-not-called, Verizon-miss → Orbi-fallback, both-miss returns ok=False without saving, both-error propagates errors list, MAC normalisation hyphen→colon) + TestMergeDeviceData additions (5 — Verizon captures dns_hostname, Orbi captures dns_hostname, ARP doesn't blank previous good name, ARP-only first sighting leaves field empty-string not undefined, etc.). 1,940 tests, 85.77% coverage. architecture.html: 67→68 endpoints, /api/homenet/device/rescan-hostname route chip, homenet.py 2,511→2,641 lines, test count 1,766→1,940. |  |

| 10 | **Device Name Resolution** ✅ SHIPPED 2026-04-23 (commit chain: original 5ddb2ac, hotfixes 0f857ae / e7d2cd4 / 5aff577 / 14e9406 / edb042c → final merge 1c8b0b1). **Live verified**: 76 devices, 0 Unknown (was 34), QNAP 3-NIC bond all green after full + light scan cycle. Three Python-first additions landed: (a) **IEEE OUI lookup** via `mac-vendor-lookup` (covers ~36k vendors from MA-L/M/S; curated `_MAC_VENDORS` dict wins for friendly short names). (b) **Locally-admin MAC detection** — bit 0x02 of first octet → "Random MAC (Phone)". (c) **mDNS / Bonjour discovery** via `zeroconf` as Phase 0 in `_resolve_names_batch`. Plus `_categorise_by_vendor_substring` (20+ vendor families) for IEEE long-form names. **Five hotfixes that each fixed a real bug**: (1) don't cache Unknown (startup-race cache poisoning); (2) `_ieee_lookup_lock` serialises mac-vendor-lookup's asyncio event loop across threads; (3) `_enrich_device_names` backfills stale Unknown vendors on every scan (offline-for-days devices never got re-resolved); (4) `_rollup_active_by_ip` aggregates active-status across MACs sharing an IP (covers LACP / balance-alb / active-backup / HA floating IPs) so bonded NICs stay consistently green; (5) light-scan path also calls the rollup so bonded NICs don't flicker every 60s as the fast ARP sweep runs. 47 tests across TestIsLocallyAdminMac / TestMacVendorIEEELookup / TestVendorCategorySubstring / TestMdnsResolveBatch / TestEnrichDeviceNames. `mac-vendor-lookup>=0.1.13` + `zeroconf>=0.132.0` in requirements.txt. |  |
| 11 | **Custom Backup Solution** | (Section 4 of #47 Backup tab) Replace Windows built-in backup (WindowsImageBackup) with a managed backup strategy integrated into WinDesktopMgr. Current pain: WindowsImageBackup silently consumed ~2.4 TB on E: drive by backing up multiple drives instead of just C:, and the built-in tools gave no visibility into what was happening. Now scoped as **Section 4 of the unified Backup tab introduced by #47**. Shows backup size/age per drive, lets user configure which drives to back up and retention policy, surfaces disk space impact on the dashboard, and alerts when backups are growing unexpectedly. Consider robocopy-based incremental backups or VSS snapshots with explicit size controls. Must provide clear visibility into what's being backed up and how much space it's using. Marked "future" by user 2026-05-24 — #47 + #46 ship first, then this. | Large | P1 |

| 14 | **System Comparison / Baseline Mode** ✅ SHIPPED 2026-04-24 (commit 5dd381b, merge 0afb0c1). **Live verified**: tray restarted, `/api/baseline/drift` reports first-run state (has_baseline=False) with correct counts scraped from the actual system (73 startup items / 327 services / 210 scheduled tasks); all three UI pieces (nav button, panel, `loadBaseline` JS) served by the live server. New `baseline.py` module (~380 lines) mirroring the `bios_audit.py` pattern: captures three security-relevant surfaces — **startup items** (HKLM/HKCU Run, Startup folders, login-triggered tasks — reuses existing `get_startup_items`), **Windows services** (psutil, Python-first), **scheduled tasks** (`schtasks /query /fo CSV /v` — no Python binding covers the full surface). Each category diffed against an accepted baseline with a focused field set: startup tracks `command` + `enabled`; services track `start_mode` + `image_path` (Auto→Disabled sabotage or rogue-binary swap); tasks track `state` + `image_path` + `run_as` (INTERACTIVE→SYSTEM privilege escalation). Four new routes: `GET /api/baseline/{drift,snapshot,history}` + `POST /api/baseline/accept`. New `📐 Baseline / Drift` top-level tab with three states: (a) no-baseline first-run → "Capture initial baseline" button + preview counts; (b) no-drift → green-check "System matches baseline"; (c) drift → categorised add/removed/changed lists per category + "Accept current as baseline" button. Dashboard concern at info level (reads append-only history file, not live compute) links to the new tab. 39 new tests in `tests/test_baseline.py` covering collectors / diff semantics / persistence / atomic-write failure / drift recording / first-run / clamp / route / dashboard-concern wiring. Files gitignored: `baseline_snapshot.json` + `baseline_history.json`. |  |

On-demand system health report generator with 3 formats (Markdown for support tickets/GitHub issues/chat, HTML for browser-rendered standalone documents, JSON for machine-readable export) + 5 scopes (full / dashboard / bsod / hardware / network) + PII redaction (default ON: MAC / IPv4 / IPv6 (both `::`-shorthand and full forms) / email / hostname / BIOS service tag). New `report.py` module + `GET /api/report/export` route + dashboard "📤 Export Report" button → modal with format/scope/redact toggles + 3 actions (📋 Copy to clipboard, 💾 Download file, 🔗 Open in tab). +44 tests (TestRedactPii × 12, TestRenderMarkdown × 7, TestRenderHtml × 5 including XSS guard, TestRenderJson × 3, TestGenerateReport × 6, TestReportExportRoute × 10 + 1 collector-failure-returns-500 guard). **Live verified post-merge**: dashboard markdown export returns 430 bytes (HTTP 200), full JSON un-redacted returns 9,427 bytes (HTTP 200). Honest scope notes: redact-PII is conservative (false-positive over-redact is much cheaper than false-negative leak); `127.0.0.1` and `0.0.0.0` are preserved (placeholders, not PII); HTML renderer does cheap inline markdown→HTML conversion to avoid runtime markdown library dep. |  |
| 16 | **Remote Reboot Routers & Switch** ✅ SHIPPED 2026-05-09 (commit 6378de6 → PR #22 → merge 32a8dc8 on origin/main). One-click reboot for the three home-network infrastructure devices via mixed approach: **Orbi RBRE960** uses real SOAP `DeviceConfig:1#Reboot` (true one-click, ConnectionError mid-response treated as success indicator); **Verizon CR1000A** uses Path A deep-link to admin `#/system/reboot` (SPA write payload buried in minified Vue chunks, probing write endpoints rejected by sandbox); **TP-Link TL-SG2218** uses Path A deep-link to web admin (SNMP reboot OID undocumented in public MIB for this model). New `_orbi_reboot_router()` + `_verizon_reboot_url()` + `_tplink_reboot_url()` helpers + `POST /api/homenet/reboot/<device>` route with TYPE-TO-CONFIRM guard (body must include `confirm` field matching device key — defense in depth so stray POSTs can't take down network without explicit intent). UI: collapsible "⚠ Remote Reboot" panel in Home Network tab with mode badges (SOAP / deep-link) + modal that requires user to type the device key exactly to enable the destructive Reboot button (auto-closes 2.5s after success, ESC cancels). +17 tests (TestRebootRoute × 8, TestOrbiRebootHelper × 5 including ConnectTimeout-vs-ConnectionError disambiguation bug-fix-with-regression-test, TestVerizonAndTpLinkRebootHelpers × 4). **Live verified post-merge**: missing `confirm` returns 400, Verizon with confirm returns `{mode:'deep-link', url:'http://[redacted-ip]/#/system/reboot', ok:true}` (no actual reboot fired). Honest scope notes: Verizon + TP-Link are NOT true one-click (2-click: dashboard confirm + admin UI Reboot); SNMP write to TP-Link reboot OID would be true one-click but isn't in published MIB. |  |

| 29 | **Frontend Rate-Limiting + Response Caching for `/api/summary/*`** ✅ SHIPPED 2026-05-22 (PR #48, merge 22757b1). Both halves landed: (1) **server-side ETag** on `/api/summary/<tab>` — strong ETag = SHA-256 of canonical response JSON salted by tab name (16 hex chars), `If-None-Match` match → `304 Not Modified` with empty body + ETag echoed; summarizer still runs every request (standard response-derived ETag) so backend state changes are never masked by a stale 304; (2) **JS-side 1-second identical-payload debounce** on `fetchSummary()` — same `JSON.stringify(payload)` within 1s skips the network call entirely; per-tab ETag cache + auto `If-None-Match`; 304 short-circuits the json()/renderSummary() round-trip. **Live verified post-merge**: live tray sets `ETag: "da5610e2e3c93b04"` on the first call and returns `304 NOT MODIFIED` with zero body on the repeat (verified via `curl -i`). 8 new tests in `TestSummaryRouteEtag` (test_routes.py) — ETag presence, 304 zero-body, stale-ETag → 200, payload→ETag distinctness, cross-tab salt, ETag stability under repeated identical input, response-content tracking (mutated state under fixed payload yields new ETag), unknown-tab 404 carries no ETag. 2,273 → 2,281 tests, 86.26% → 86.29% coverage. `dev.py verify` all-green. Pairs with #34 (ETag on /api/scan/results) which can now follow the same pattern. |  |
| 30 | **Parallelize pytest with pytest-xdist** ✅ SHIPPED 2026-04-24. **Live measured: serial 216.9s → parallel 47.9s (4.5x), or 54s via `python dev.py test` end-to-end with overhead. All 1,813 tests pass, coverage stays at 85.13%.** `pytest-xdist>=3.5.0` added to `requirements-dev.txt` + `pyproject.toml [project.optional-dependencies] dev`. Wired into the inner dev loop in three places: (1) `dev.py cmd_test` runs `pytest tests/ -n auto`, (2) the `pytest` pre-commit hook now uses `-n auto -x -q --no-header`, (3) CLAUDE.md SOP step 3 + the Quality Gates + Running Tests sections all reference `pytest -n auto`. Default `pytest tests/` (no -n) stays serial — no breaking change for anyone running plain pytest. Pre-commit `pytest-integration` hook stays SERIAL (real PowerShell / WMI / nvidia-smi can't parallelise; rate limits + exclusive Windows resources). Audit confirmed every persistent test file (BASELINE_FILE, HISTORY_FILE, ALERT_RULES_FILE, METRICS HISTORY_FILE, REMEDIATION_HISTORY_FILE) is monkey-patched to `tmp_path` which xdist isolates per worker; module-level globals reset by autouse `reset_globals` fixture are per-process so workers don't step on each other. |  |
| 31 | **Index `/api/logs` reads by level + date-hour** | Filtered `/api/logs?level=WARNING` now scans the full 10 MB rotating file every call (fixed 2026-04-20 so warnings aren't buried under INFO floods). Fine for the Logs tab (rare, manual). If the dashboard ever starts polling logs we'd want a (level, date-hour) offset index — build on startup + append on each write — so filtered reads become O(matches). No urgency unless the dashboard starts polling. | Medium | P3 |
| 32 | **Persistent PowerShell Runspace for Hot Paths** | Each PS subprocess spawn costs 200–500 ms of process startup before any real work happens. A long-lived PS host process that accepts commands over a pipe / named-socket could eliminate that overhead for hot paths (dashboard collectors, metrics sampler). Complexity is real — restart handling, timeout semantics, command isolation, protocol design — and the hot-path PS calls are already handled by backlog #24's Python migration. **Pick up only if a specific perf complaint lands that isn't addressable by backlog #24/#28.** | Large | P3 |
| 33 | **SQLite-backed `metrics_history` once Phase 2 grows metric cardinality** | Current implementation reads + rewrites the full JSON file on each sample (~1 MB, microseconds at current scale). Fine for 5 metrics × 1 sample / 10 min × 27 days ≈ 4 000 entries. If Phase 2 adds per-process memory sampling (50 procs × 1 sample / min × 30 days ≈ 2 M entries, ~500 MB of JSON) we'd need SQLite — still embedded, no daemon, indexed range queries. Escalation path: JSON → SQLite → real TSDB. **Pick up when we start adding per-entity metrics.** | Medium | P3 |

| 44 | **Baseline drift — unified cross-surface change view + correlation detection** ✅ SHIPPED 2026-05-22 (PR #49, merge a09ff88). See Done-table row for full scope. Live system has a real 21-event 3-category cluster that the new timeline surfaces as critical and the dashboard concern flags at warning. Follow-up to #14 (System Comparison / Baseline Mode, shipped). Original scope: today the Baseline / Drift tab shows three independent per-category lists — startup adds/removed/changed, services adds/removed/changed, scheduled-tasks adds/removed/changed. That makes single-surface changes easy to scan but hides the security-relevant pattern: a single install / Windows Update / malware event typically touches **multiple** surfaces within seconds of each other (e.g. a service registers + drops a scheduled task + adds a Run-key entry, all timestamped within 30 s). Today the user has to mentally join three lists and squint at timestamps to see that. **Build a unified change-timeline view** that joins all three baseline categories into one chronologically-sorted feed of change events (one row per add/remove/change with category badge + timestamp + diff summary), grouped into "change clusters" when ≥2 events of different categories land within a configurable window (default 5 min). Each cluster gets a single header row summarising its scope ("3 events in 12 s: 1 service, 1 task, 1 startup") with the per-event rows underneath; lone events stay ungrouped. Cluster headers carry a severity hint based on how many categories the cluster touches (1 = info, 2 = warning, 3 = critical — a single event that touches startup + services + tasks is the canonical malware-install signature). **Data shape**: `baseline_history.json` already records timestamped drift entries per category — just needs a new `correlate_drift_events(history, window_seconds)` helper that flattens entries to a single sorted event stream and runs a single-pass sliding-window cluster detector. **UI**: new "📊 Change Timeline" sub-panel above the existing per-category lists on the Baseline/Drift tab; toggle to switch between "Timeline" and "By Category" views. New `GET /api/baseline/timeline?window=300` route returning the clustered event stream. Add a dashboard concern at warning level when the most-recent cluster touched ≥3 categories within 60 s — this is the "I want to know NOW" signal. Tests: pure correlator (single-cluster, multi-cluster, lone events, window-edge boundaries, empty history, malformed entries) + route (default window, custom window, query-param bounds, empty case) + dashboard-concern wiring. Honest scope notes: baseline diffs are append-only and re-snapshotted on demand, so the timeline only sees changes the user has captured — won't catch installs between snapshots. Still strictly better than today's three-list view. |  |
| 45 | **Remote access to the dashboard from mobile / laptop (off-LAN)** | Today the tray runs `app.run(port=5000)` which Flask binds to `127.0.0.1` only -- the UI is reachable from the desktop browser and nothing else. There is also **no auth on any endpoint** (`@app.before_request` is logging only), so exposing port 5000 to the network today would let anything on the LAN call `/api/processes/kill`, `/api/remediation/*`, `/api/homenet/reboot/<device>`, etc. Goal: let the user open the WinDesktopMgr UI from their phone at a coffee shop or laptop while traveling, without making the home network a target. Two prerequisites + three deployment options. **Prerequisite A — auth gate (NON-NEGOTIABLE):** add a per-install bearer-token check in a `@app.before_request` hook, token generated on first run and stored in `%APPDATA%\WinDesktopMgr\auth_token` (only readable by the tray's user); browser sends it as `Authorization: Bearer <token>` (or via cookie set by an unauthenticated `/login` POST). Test paths must continue to work via a `TESTING=True` bypass. Localhost requests can bypass auth too so the existing local UX stays unchanged. **Prerequisite B — destructive-action confirmation gates:** routes that today have UI-side confirmation only (kill_process, reboot/<device>, the remediation runners) should ALSO require an explicit `confirm_token` body field that the UI fills from the freshly-displayed entry. The `/api/homenet/reboot/<device>` route already has this pattern via PR #22; extend it to kill_process + the remediation runners. **Option 1 — Tailscale (RECOMMENDED for personal use):** install Tailscale on the desktop + phone + laptop; all three get 100.x addresses; browse to `http://shigs78-pc24:5000` from any device. No port forward, no public DNS, no inbound exposure on the home network. Tailscale handles WireGuard transport + the SSO. Just bind Flask to `0.0.0.0` (so the Tailscale interface accepts connections) instead of localhost. Smallest blast radius, smallest setup cost. **Option 2 — Cloudflare Tunnel:** run `cloudflared tunnel` on the desktop, expose at `mgmt.<userdomain>.com`, gate with Cloudflare Access (SSO/MFA). No port forward, no inbound exposure on the home network. Adds a public DNS name + requires a domain the user controls. Browser-only — no client app to install on the phone. **Option 3 — Native port-forward + Let's Encrypt:** classic but most attack surface. Forward 443 on the Verizon CR1000A to the desktop, get a Let's Encrypt cert via the existing `certbot` flow or a built-in `acme.py` script, dynamic-DNS via Cloudflare or similar. Auth gate becomes the only thing between the open internet and the dashboard. Not recommended given the destructive endpoints. **Suggested scope for V1:** ship the auth gate + the destructive-action `confirm_token` extension + a Tailscale-friendly "bind to 0.0.0.0 when env var `WDM_REMOTE=1` is set" config flag. Option 2 / Option 3 documented in a follow-up doc but not built into the app. **Mobile UX:** the dashboard tab + Home Network tab + Baseline tab are the highest-value views on a small screen; the rest can fall through to the existing responsive layout. Maybe a `?compact=1` query flag that hides the SVG diagrams and stacks the cards vertically — Phase 2 polish only. **Tests:** auth-gate test matrix (no token → 401, wrong token → 401, right token → 200, localhost-bypass → 200, TESTING flag → 200 without token); destructive-confirm extension tests for the three new gated endpoints; new live-verify check that the auth path actually rejects unauthenticated requests when `WDM_REMOTE=1` is set. **Honest scope notes:** Tailscale is third-party software (acceptable for a personal tool; would be a non-starter for fleet deployment); the auth-gate doesn't itself give MFA (Tailscale or Cloudflare Access provides that); browser-stored bearer token is XSS-vulnerable so the UI must continue to escape every external string (existing `escHtml` discipline applies). | Medium–Large | P2 |
| 46 | ⏳ **OneDrive → iCloud file replicator** | (Section 3 of #47 Backup tab) ⏳ PR-1 + PR-2 SHIPPED 2026-05-24/25 (PRs #53 / #55) — rule editor + preview + actual copy engine + crash-safe resume + schedule SCHEMA (OFF by default). PR-3 (4-parallel + Windows Task Scheduler wiring) still pending. Re-scoped 2026-05-24 after spec session. One-way file replicator that pushes OneDrive contents into iCloud Drive so the same files are reachable from iPhone / iPad / Mac without giving up OneDrive as the primary store. **NOT a sync** — uni-directional. **NOT a separate tab** — Section 3 of the unified Backup tab introduced by #47. Both clouds already present on this box: OneDrive sync client + `icloudservices` are tracked in the process glossary; OneDrive at `C:\Users\<user>\OneDrive`, iCloud Drive at `C:\Users\<user>\iCloudDrive`. **V1 spec (locked 2026-05-24):** _(Q1 — Scope)_ Default = mirror **everything** under OneDrive. User-configurable **EXCLUSION rules** saved to `cloudcopy_rules.json` covering three exclude dimensions: (a) folder paths (e.g. exclude `OneDrive/Work-Confidential/`, exclude `OneDrive/Pictures/Screenshots/`), (b) file extensions (e.g. exclude `.tmp`, `.bak`, `.iso`), (c) filename glob patterns (e.g. exclude `~$*` Office lock files, `Thumbs.db`, `.DS_Store`, `*.partial`). All three dimensions are exclude-only — the model is "include everything, then strike out what you don't want", not "pick what you want." Personal Vault is excluded by default (encrypted at rest by OneDrive; copying sideways defeats the protection) but the user can opt-in via a Settings toggle. _(Q2 — Conflict)_ **Source-wins (always overwrite)** when content hash differs. OneDrive is the source of truth; iCloud is the downstream mirror. Caveat written into the UI: "Edits made on the iCloud side will be overwritten on the next run." _(Q3 — Photos)_ **Files only, same directory structure as OneDrive** — photo files land in the mirrored folder tree (e.g. `iCloudDrive/Pictures/2026/05/foo.heic`), NOT in the Photos.app library. UI banner explains: "Photos appear in iPhone Files.app, not Photos.app." Photos.app library integration is explicitly out of scope; not even a Phase 2 commitment unless the user revisits. _(Q4 — Trigger)_ **Manual + optional schedule**. Big "Start Cloud Copy" button on Section 3 with type-to-confirm guard (mirrors PR #22 reboot). Opt-in nightly schedule (default 2 AM via Windows Task Scheduler) that runs the saved exclusion rules. **THE SCHEDULE MUST BE OFF BY DEFAULT** (user-confirmed 2026-05-25): first-run UI shows the toggle in the disabled state with a "Schedule disabled — click to enable" label; no Windows Task Scheduler entry is created until the user explicitly clicks Enable + confirms a time; persists in `cloudcopy_rules.json` as `schedule_enabled: false` baked into DEFAULT_RULES. File-watcher / near-real-time mirror is a SEPARATE backlog item (see #48). **Core mechanic**: walk OneDrive tree → for each non-excluded file, materialise Files-On-Demand placeholder via `attrib +p` (same pin trick used by the March 2026 SystemHealthDiag.py path-fix), copy to mirrored path under `iCloudDrive\OneDrive-Mirror\` (root prefix configurable), skip when destination size + mtime + content-hash all match. Atomic-rename via `.tmp` staging on the destination so a crash mid-copy never leaves a half-written file for iCloud's sync client to ship up. **Crash recovery / resume** (added 2026-05-24 per user): every copy session must survive a mid-run crash (tray killed, machine BSOD, power loss, iCloud Drive going offline) and resume from where it left off — not restart from scratch. Two files cooperate: (a) `cloudcopy_state.json` — the IN-PROGRESS state for the currently-running session: session id, started_at, plan (the full list of files the rules selected, with their indexes), cursor (index of the next file to attempt), per-file commit log of what's been successfully copied so far. Written atomically (`.tmp` + `os.replace`) AFTER each individual file's destination-side atomic-rename completes, NOT before — so the worst case after a crash is "one file was half-copied and skipped"; on resume that file's destination hash won't match the source hash and the file gets retried automatically. (b) `cloudcopy_history.json` — append-only log of EVERY past session including crashed ones, each entry shaped `{session_id, started_at, ended_at crashed_at, status: "completed\|cancelled\|crashed", files_planned, files_completed, files_failed, bytes_copied, errors[], resumed_from_session_id?}`. On tray startup, the worker thread checks `cloudcopy_state.json`: if an in-progress session exists (no clean "completed" marker), it's marked `crashed` in history with whatever the last-committed cursor was, and a **"Previous run crashed at file 1,247 / 8,902 (14 %, 23.4 GB copied). Resume?"** banner surfaces on Section 3 with two buttons: **Resume** (re-validates that the source files still exist + the iCloud destination is still mounted + the rules file hasn't changed; on green light, continues from the saved cursor) and **Discard** (writes a `cancelled` history entry, clears the state file, returns to idle). Resume is the recommended action; Discard is for the case where the source has materially changed since the crash. **Rate-limit safety**: cap parallel copies at 4 by default, exponential backoff on transient I/O errors, soft-cap on total bytes per session (default 50 GB) so an unattended schedule can't exhaust iCloud quota. **UI**: Section 3 of Backup tab. Six panels: (a) Status header — last run time, files copied / skipped / failed, current state (idle / running / cancelled / crashed / errored); **(b) Resume banner (NEW, conditional)** — visible ONLY when a crashed-session state file is found on tray startup; shows "Previous run crashed at file N / M (P %, X GB copied) at T" with **Resume** and **Discard** buttons; auto-dismisses on either click; (c) Exclusion rules editor — three sub-sections (folders, extensions, filename globs) with add / edit / delete + persistence to `cloudcopy_rules.json`; (d) Preview — "Click to preview what would be copied with current rules" → returns file count + total bytes + sample of first 50 included paths + sample of first 50 excluded paths; (e) Run controls — Start (type-to-confirm), Cancel (cooperative flag for in-progress run), Schedule toggle + time picker; (f) Run history — append-only log of past sessions with start/end timestamps, file counts, bytes copied, errors, and a **crashed-vs-completed-vs-cancelled status pill** so the user can spot stability issues over time. **API**: `GET /api/cloudcopy/rules` (read rules), `PUT /api/cloudcopy/rules` (save rules, validates exclusion shape), `GET /api/cloudcopy/preview` (count + bytes + sample paths using current rules), `POST /api/cloudcopy/run` (type-to-confirm guard; kicks off a session in a worker thread; returns session id), `GET /api/cloudcopy/status?session=...` (live progress), `POST /api/cloudcopy/cancel?session=...` (cooperative cancel), `GET /api/cloudcopy/history` (last N sessions with completed / crashed / cancelled status), `POST /api/cloudcopy/schedule` (enable/disable + cron time), **`GET /api/cloudcopy/resume-state` (returns the crashed-session info, or `{has_crashed: false}` if none)**, **`POST /api/cloudcopy/resume` (continues from `cloudcopy_state.json` cursor; re-validates source + destination first; writes a `resumed_from_session_id` link into history)**, **`POST /api/cloudcopy/discard-crashed` (writes a `cancelled` history entry, clears the state file)**. **Tests**: pure rule-evaluator (3 exclude dimensions × precedence × glob edge cases × trailing slash + case-sensitivity quirks); pure conflict resolver (source-wins behaviour for added/changed/identical/missing destinations); placeholder-materialisation mock (Files-On-Demand `attrib +p`); atomic-rename + `.tmp`-staging happy + crash paths; rate-limit + backoff harness; **crash-recovery suite (NEW, first-class): (i) state file written after each file commit, NOT before; (ii) orphaned state file on startup → history gains a `crashed` entry with correct files_completed count; (iii) resume re-runs the file that was mid-copy when the crash hit (its `.tmp` destination is cleaned up before resume); (iv) resume revalidates source-file existence + destination-mount + rules-unchanged before continuing, falls back to a clear error if any fail; (v) resumed session's history entry carries `resumed_from_session_id`; (vi) discard-crashed writes a cancelled entry and clears state; (vii) crash during cancel-in-progress is also handled cleanly; (viii) two crashes back-to-back chain correctly (resume of resume of original)**; route matrix (preview, run, status, cancel, history, schedule, rules CRUD, resume-state, resume, discard-crashed, type-to-confirm guard); Playwright smoke for the rule editor + preview + start flow + crash-recovery banner appear-and-dismiss. **Honest scope notes**: (a) Uni-directional only — iCloud-side edits get wiped on next run; user's mental model = "iCloud is a downstream mirror of OneDrive." (b) Not a sync — for two-way the user reaches for a third-party tool (MultCloud, GoodSync); building that is explicitly out of scope. (c) Copying enterprise-managed (Business / EDU) OneDrive content into a personal iCloud account may violate the user's organisation's data-handling policy — V1 doesn't enforce that, but a one-line banner on Section 3 ("Personal data only — don't copy work-tenanted OneDrive content") is cheap + honest. (d) iCloud quota error mid-run = log + abort the session cleanly; user gets a clear error message in the run history. | Medium–Large | P2 |

Surface per-snapshot size, age, source drives, target drive, last successful run, and a "what's actually inside this" peek (catalog read, no extraction). Health checks: orphan snapshots, broken catalog, unexpected growth, multi-drive backups when single-drive was intended. No write actions in V1 — pure visibility, since the underlying tools (`wbadmin`) are the canonical way to clean these up and reproducing that surface is out of scope. **(2) Existing File History** — read-only inventory of the per-file rolling backup that's been in Windows since 8. Surface enabled/disabled state, configured target drive, included libraries, excluded folders, last-run timestamp, total versions stored, total bytes, oldest retained version. Health checks: target drive offline, retention growing unbounded, included folders that moved + are no longer reachable, last-run > 7 days ago. Again no write actions in V1. **(3) OneDrive → iCloud replicator** — see #46 for the locked V1 spec (folders + extensions + filename-glob excludes, source-wins overwrite, files-only with same directory structure as OneDrive, manual + optional schedule). **(4) Custom Backup Solution (future)** — see #11 for the originally-planned managed backup story (drive selection + retention + size visibility + disk-impact dashboard concern); explicitly future per user. **Tab chrome (this item's V1 scope)**: new `📦 Backup` top-level tab with a four-section vertical layout. Each section is collapsible (open by default for sections 1+2+3 once they ship; section 4 stays collapsed with a "Coming later" pill until #11 ships). Section headers show a status pill (green/orange/red) summarising health for that section so the user sees the whole story at a glance without expanding everything. **V1 deliverables for #47 specifically (NOT including #46 or #11):** the tab itself + sections 1 + 2 (read-only inventory + health checks). New `backup.py` module mirroring the `baseline.py` pattern (collectors, health-checker functions, atomic JSON cache for the slow `wbadmin get versions` parse). New routes: `GET /api/backup/windows-backups` (catalog list + per-snapshot metadata), `GET /api/backup/file-history` (state + config + retention summary), `GET /api/backup/summary` (single-call dashboard fan-out aggregating sections 1+2 health). Existing baseline-style dashboard concern wiring: if any section reports critical-level health (e.g. File History target offline, WindowsImageBackup growing >50 % week-over-week), emit a `warning`-level concern at the dashboard level. **Backend details (Section 1)**: parse `wbadmin get versions` (no real Python binding for Windows Server Backup; subprocess to wbadmin.exe is the KEEP-class path per docs/powershell_audit.md). Cache the result in `backup_cache.json` with a 1-hour TTL because the catalog read is slow (~5-15 s on a populated catalog). **Backend details (Section 2)**: parse `fhmanagew.exe -getconfig` for current state OR read the underlying `Configuration\Config1.xml` from the user's File History directory (the XML path is faster + doesn't need elevation; fall back to fhmanagew if the XML can't be located). **Tests**: pure parsers for both wbadmin output and File History XML (fixtures from real outputs on this machine + edge cases — empty catalog, disabled-File-History, target-drive-offline); health-checker thresholds (per-section critical/warning/info); route tests (200 / 404 / shape stability); dashboard-concern wiring tests (fires when section reports critical; doesn't when sections are healthy). **Cleanup / delete actions (added 2026-05-24 per user)**: V1 also includes safe-by-default cleanup paths so the user can attack the 2.4 TB WindowsImageBackup problem from inside the tab. Two write actions: (a) **Section 1 — delete a specific WindowsImageBackup version** via `wbadmin delete backup -version:<id>` (or `-deleteOldest -keepVersions:N`). UI flow: pick a version → preview "this will free X GB, leaves N versions" → TYPE-TO-CONFIRM gate (mirrors PR #22) → subprocess kicks off with UAC elevation prompt (Windows shows the consent UI; tray stays unelevated). (b) **Section 2 — File History cleanup** via `fhmanagew.exe -cleanup <days>` (deletes versions older than N days; `-cleanup 0` keeps only the newest). Same preview + type-to-confirm + UAC flow. Both actions append to a new `backup_actions_history.json` (append-only log of what was deleted, when, bytes freed) so the dashboard can show "47 GB freed in last 30 days" and the user can audit their own cleanup. Hard-blocked safety rails: (i) refuse to delete the most-recent backup version (always keep ≥1); (ii) refuse if the target drive is currently the active backup destination AND a backup is in progress (parse `wbadmin get status`); (iii) refuse if File History is currently mid-cleanup; (iv) all subprocess calls captured (stdout + stderr + returncode) into the action log so failures are visible, not silent. **Sequencing within #47**: PR-1 ships read-only inventory + tab chrome + dashboard concerns first (low risk, validates the parsers against real data). PR-2 adds the cleanup/delete actions on top once the read paths are proven. PR-3 onwards (separate items) brings in #46 Section 3 and #11 Section 4. **Honest scope notes**: cleanup is INVOCATION-only — the tab shells out to Microsoft's own `wbadmin` and `fhmanagew` tools; we don't reimplement the catalog logic. If those tools fail or change behaviour across Windows updates, the failure surfaces in the action log with full stdout/stderr. |  |

Reuses #46's exclusion rules, source-wins conflict mode, atomic-rename, and `cloudcopy_state.json` resume support. **Why deferred**: long-running worker thread + complex failure modes (watcher dies silently, queue backlog under heavy churn, file in-use during copy attempt, watcher fires before OneDrive's own sync finishes downloading the placeholder); easier to validate the V1 manual + schedule path first, then layer the watcher on top once the core mechanic is proven. **Pick-up criteria**: only after #46 has been live for ≥2 weeks AND there's a concrete user complaint that "I edited X in OneDrive but it didn't show up in iCloud until midnight." If the scheduled run is good enough, this item never needs to ship. **Test additions over V1**: watcher restart on tray reboot, debounce window correctness, queue overflow handling, watcher-fires-during-active-copy race, watcher-dies-silently detection + restart, exclusion-rules-change-mid-watch reload. Same exclusion rules + conflict + atomic-rename guarantees as #46 V1 — only the trigger differs. | Medium | P3 |

History: ⏳ PR 1 SHIPPED 2026-10-12 (PR #206) — kernel_cpu.py (ntdll per-thread CPU of PID 4, unelevated) + dashboard warning (>50% of a core for 2 intervals >=20 s) + Temps tab 'Kernel driver load' line (live: busiest 10.6%, normal) + LHM 30 s last-good cache + real-call selftest (15 checks). The stuck alert itself is not yet seen firing live (no stuck driver since Killer was fixed). ⏳ PR 2 SHIPPED 2026-10-12 (PR #207) — UAC 'Identify driver' button (scripts/kernel_cpu_elevated.py, EnumDeviceDrivers + thread StartAddress, known-driver hints); unelevated fallback verified live; AWAITING the user's first elevated click to see real driver names before closing.  -- found 2026-10-11 while diagnosing 'cores persistently over 70 C': Intel Killer Traffic Control (KfeCo11X64.sys, Killer Prioritization Engine) had ONE System-process thread spinning at 99.5% of a core 24/7. Windows parked that work on the favored P-cores (#5/#6 at 5.5-6 GHz, ~1.4 V) -> 68-83 C at ~5% load and ~60 W idle package power; after turning the engine off: kernel 119% -> 17% of a core, hottest P-core ~59 C, quiet floor ~42 W. Nothing in the app could see it: Get-Process can't read System/protected processes' CPU, and the Thermals tab only shows temps. **Design (proven in a throwaway diagnosis script):** NtQuerySystemInformation(SystemProcessInformation, class 5) gives every System (PID 4) thread's kernel+user time WITHOUT opening threads (works unelevated); snapshot twice ~30 s apart, flag any single thread > ~50% of a core sustained across 2+ samples (a 'stuck driver thread', distinct from normal ntoskrnl worker churn). Naming the driver needs admin: thread StartAddress + EnumDeviceDrivers base addresses are hidden unelevated -> name it via the existing UAC elevated-helper pattern (scripts/backup_helper_elevated.py) on a 'Identify driver' button, else say 'a kernel driver'. Concern text links the hot core to it (Thermals + dashboard), known-offender hint table (KfeCo*/Killer -> turn off Prioritization Engine). Read-only; tests mock the ntdll call. Side finding to fold in: LHM's data.json sometimes stalls 5-20 s, so the Thermals tab flickers 'no sensor' -- cache the last good LHM reading for ~30 s. |  |

| 2026-05-22 | Tech Debt | ETag/304 + 1s debounce on `/api/summary/*` (PR #48, backlog #29) | Defensive follow-up to the 2026-04-20 flood incident where a stuck browser tab fired `POST /api/summary/drivers` ~100×/sec. Two independent defenses: (1) **server-side ETag** — `/api/summary/<tab>` now sets a strong ETag (SHA-256 of canonical response JSON, salted by tab name to prevent cross-tab replay); matching `If-None-Match` → `304` with zero body and ETag echoed; summarizer still runs on every request (response-derived ETag, standard HTTP semantics) so backend state changes are never masked. (2) **JS-side 1-second identical-payload debounce** on `fetchSummary()` — if the same `JSON.stringify(payload)` is fired again within 1s the network call is skipped entirely; per-tab ETag cache + automatic `If-None-Match` on subsequent fetches; on 304 the json()/renderSummary() round-trip is skipped. **Live verified post-merge**: `curl -X POST /api/summary/updates` returns `ETag: "da5610e2e3c93b04"`; the repeat with `If-None-Match` returns `304 NOT MODIFIED` (zero body). +8 tests (`TestSummaryRouteEtag`). 2,273 → 2,281 tests, 86.26% → 86.29% coverage; `dev.py verify` all-green first try. |
| 2026-05-22 | Feature | Baseline drift — cross-surface correlation timeline (PR #49, backlog #44) | New unified change-timeline view on the Baseline / Drift tab. Flattens the three per-category drift lists into one chronologically-sorted feed and clusters multi-category bursts within a configurable window (default 5 min). Cluster severity scales with the number of categories touched: 1 = info (benign single-surface churn), 2 = warning, 3 = critical (canonical install / malware fingerprint). Same-category bursts stay ungrouped — the correlation signal is cross-surface coincidence, not same-surface volume. New `GET /api/baseline/timeline?window=<seconds>` route (clamped 10..86400). New **warning-level dashboard concern** fires when the most-recent cluster touched ≥3 categories within 60 s, distinct from the existing info-level "drift detected in 24h" concern. UI: new "📊 Change Timeline" panel above the per-category lists with a window selector (60 s / 5 min / 15 min / 1 h); cluster panel border color tracks severity (orange/red); per-event drill-down rows inside each cluster. **Live verified post-merge**: API returns 14 critical clusters from the user's actual history (real 21-event, 180-event, 68-event multi-surface bursts), dashboard concern fires at warning level with the right deep-link. +24 tests: `TestCorrelateDriftEvents` ×12 (empty, malformed, lone, same-cat ungrouped, 3-cat critical, 2-cat warning, window boundary inclusive at exactly N seconds + exclusive at N+1, newest-first order, delta on changed, idempotent, internal `_ts` stripped), `TestCorrelationAlert` ×5 (no-history, lone events, 2-cat below threshold, 3-cat trips alert, picks most-recent cluster), `TestBaselineTimelineRoute` ×5 (empty, custom window, clamp [10, 86400], garbage falls back, real history clusters), `TestBaselineDashboardConcern` extended ×2 (cross-surface concern fires for 3-cat; doesn't fire for 2-cat). 2,281 → 2,305 tests, 86.29% → 86.28% coverage (fractional). architecture.html: +1 route chip (`/api/baseline/timeline`), `test_baseline.py` chip 39 → 138 tests + "cross-surface correlation timeline + cluster severity" phrase, `baseline.py` file-system sub-label updated to mention the cross-surface correlation timeline + dashboard concerns (info + warning), total badge bumped to 2,305. `dev.py verify` all-green first try. the unelevated tray polls `/api/backup/scan-status` until the helper exits. **Three whitelisted actions**: `scan_catalog` (wbadmin get versions + WindowsImageBackup folder walk for sizes, writes `backup_cache.json`), `delete_version` (wbadmin delete backup -version:<id>), `fh_cleanup` (fhmanagew.exe -cleanup <days>). **Safety rails**: per-action validators refuse delete-most-recent, delete-only-remaining, FH-cleanup days outside [0, 3650]; type-to-confirm on every destructive action (mirrors PR #22 router-reboot guard); confirm_token must equal version_id for delete and literal "CLEANUP <days>" for FH cleanup; defense-in-depth re-validation in the elevated helper; action whitelist enforced both pre-launch and inside the helper; path-traversal guard on session_id; ephemeral request/result files cleaned after UI consumes them. **Audit log**: every elevated action appends to `backup_actions_history.json` (200-row cap, newest-first) with returncode + stdout/stderr tails + bytes_freed_estimate; | 2026-05-25 | Bug Fix | Accept-cluster: snapshot once + look up current_value for added/changed (PR #63, follow-up to #50) | User clicked Accept-all on a 213-event cluster and got "Accepted 27 of 213 changes, 186 failed" with the error `current_value (dict) required for kind='added'`. **Root cause**: JS `bl_acceptCluster` sends `current_value=null` for every event (the cluster timeline payload deliberately strips down to {category, kind, key, name, delta} to keep size sane on 200+ event clusters), but `accept_drift_entry`'s fast path requires `current_value` for added/changed kinds. Only the 27 `removed`-kind events succeeded. **Why not pass current_value through the timeline?** Would bloat the JSON payload N× for large clusters. **Why not call slow-path per-event?** That re-snapshots the system per call (~5-10s each) -- 213 events × 7s = **~25 minutes** of UI freeze. Unacceptable. **Fix**: take ONE snapshot at the start of `accept_cluster_events`, look up each added/changed event's `current_value` from `snapshot[category]["by_key"][key]`, then call `accept_drift_entry` fast-path for each. One snapshot ≈ 5-10s total regardless of event count. Pure-removed clusters skip the snapshot entirely. Stale items (user already removed since drift fired, or Windows auto-cleaned) record a soft failure with a clear reason (`"no longer present in current snapshot..."`) rather than writing a bad baseline entry. **Live verified post-deploy**: ran the fix against a real April 27 12-event cluster from the user's history → **8 of 12 accepted (66.7%)**, vs the original 12.7% (27/213). The 4 failures are stale `SoftLanding` tasks legitimately not on the system anymore; error messages are now actionable instead of cryptic. **+4 regression tests** in TestAcceptClusterEvents: snapshot-lookup-populates-current-value (asserts ONE snapshot regardless of event count), snapshot-skipped-when-all-events-have-value, added-event-not-in-snapshot-soft-fails, removed-kind-skips-snapshot. 2,546 → 2,550 tests, no coverage delta. `dev.py verify` all-green. |
| 2026-05-25 | Bug Fix | Cluster Examine: add Update Session + Event Log sources (PR #59, follow-up to #50) | User clicked Examine on their first cluster (71 events) and got an empty modal: 0 Windows Updates, 0 BIOS audit changes. **Root cause**: `Get-HotFix` only returns legacy hotfixes registered with Windows Installer service; modern updates (cumulative, driver, feature, Defender definition) don't appear there. We already had the better source (`Microsoft.Update.Session.QueryHistory` via win32com, from PR #42's backlog #28 Batch G migration) but only wired into the Updates tab. **Fix**: extended `gather_cluster_context()` with two new sources: (1) **Update Session history** via the existing `windesktopmgr.get_update_history()` — catches everything `Get-HotFix` misses, returns `{title, result, operation, date, description}` per row; (2) **Event Log (Setup + Application + System)** via `_query_event_log_xpath` with a `timediff()` XPath bounded to the cluster window (20 events/channel, 6s timeout, message truncated to 300 chars to prevent 50KB modal payloads). Setup channel catches MSI installer events (1033/1034/1036/11707/11708/11724) that don't register as hotfixes — the biggest miss in the original two-source design. **Modal UX**: 4 tables now, ordered most-helpful-first (Update history → Event Log → Hotfixes → BIOS audit); per-source empty messages explain WHY a source is empty ("Get-HotFix only catches legacy hotfixes; check Update history below"); a top-level "No external signals correlate" banner appears only when ALL four sources return zero, explaining likely causes (Click-to-Run / MSIX / portable installers / admin push / manual config change) and pointing at per-entry drilldowns. **Live verified post-merge**: probed the user's May 14 cluster (66 events at 07:41:41) with a wider window — now surfaces **7 Intel SoftwareComponent Driver Update entries** from Update Session, exactly the "what happened around then" signal the original modal couldn't see. May 25 clusters (created during dev) still show zero context because there genuinely was no WU/Event Log activity in those windows — confirms the empty state is real, not a code issue. No new tests (existing TestGatherClusterContext covers response shape; new sources patched in conftest where deterministic mocking matters). `dev.py verify` all-green. |

Reproduced headlessly: clicking × visually updated the UI + in-memory `_ccRules` but did NOT call `cc_saveRules`, so on next page reload the deleted rule came back. **Root cause**: asymmetric save handling — the `+` button onclick chained `; cc_saveRules()` while the `×` button onclick had no such chain. Easy to miss because the save was at the call site rather than inside the helper. **Fix**: centralised the save call inside both `cc_addItem` and `cc_removeItem` themselves; dropped the trailing `; cc_saveRules()` from the three + button onclicks (now redundant); added `onkeydown="Enter"` handler on all three add-inputs so pressing Enter is equivalent to clicking +. Future call sites can't forget to save because the helpers own it. **New regression-protection test** (`TestCloudCopyListEditorPersists` ×3 in test_playwright_smoke.py): fetches `/api/cloudcopy/rules` AFTER each +/×/Enter interaction and asserts the server reflects the change. | 2026-05-25 | Feature | OneDrive → iCloud replicator — PR-2: copy engine + crash-safe resume + schedule schema (OFF by default) (PR #55, backlog #46) | Second of three staged PRs for #46. PR-1 shipped rule editor + preview; PR-2 ships the actual copy. **Schedule is OFF by default** (user-confirmed contract 2026-05-25): `schedule_enabled=False` + `schedule_time="02:00"` baked into DEFAULT_RULES, validated by `test_schedule_enabled_default_false`. PR-3 will wire the real Windows Task Scheduler entry; the toggle in the UI is currently a disabled "Schedule (PR-3)" placeholder with explicit "disabled by default" label so the user can see the contract is honoured. **Copy engine**: atomic per-file commit (`src → dst.tmp → fsync → os.replace`) with state file written AFTER each commit; source-wins conflict mode with size+mtime skip (1s tolerance for FS skew); cooperative cancel via threading.Event; 50 GB session cap; per-file failure isolation. **Crash-safe resume**: orphan `cloudcopy_state.json` triggers a banner; Resume re-validates source + destination + rules-hash before continuing (refuses if rules changed mid-session so half-runs under different rules can't slip through); Discard writes a "discarded" history entry + clears state. **5 new routes** (`POST /run` type-to-confirm "START CLOUD COPY"; `GET /status`; `POST /cancel`; `POST /resume`; `POST /discard-crashed`) + `GET /resume-state` updated to distinguish "session RUNNING" from "session CRASHED" so the UI doesn't show a spurious resume banner during a normal copy. **UI**: Start button enabled with type-to-confirm; live progress bar + per-file counts + cancel button mid-run; resume banner with Resume/Discard buttons; on tab load, picks up an in-flight session if user navigated away + back (no double-start). **+45 tests** across 9 classes (TestScheduleSchema ×7 including explicit default-OFF guard, TestRulesHash ×3, TestAtomicCopy ×4, TestNeedsCopy ×4, TestCopySession ×6 against synthetic tree, TestSessionLifecycle ×3, TestResumeFlow ×6 covering rules-change + missing-source refusals, TestCloudCopyPR2Routes ×10, TestResumeStateRouteDistinguishesActive ×2). 2,481 → 2,526 tests, 86.52% → 86.58% coverage. **Diagnostic bug caught during dev**: full pytest suite hung silently on every threading test → `threading.Lock` is non-reentrant and `_append_history` was acquiring `_file_lock` then calling `load_history` which re-acquired → switched to `threading.RLock`. The "tests hang with 0-byte output" symptom was a real signal of a deadlock that would have made the first real copy run wedge. **Live verified post-merge**: `schedule_enabled` is False on the live tray, all 5 new routes respond with the right shapes, type-to-confirm rejects wrong tokens with the exact error string, Backup tab visibility test (added 2026-05-25) still passes for backup. `dev.py verify` all-green first try. |

TAB_IDS extended to include "backup" (a no-op addition that should have happened in PR #50). **Proof-of-catch**: same test run against the broken tray BEFORE this PR deployed FAILED on `[chromium-backup]` with the exact diagnostic "#page-backup is still display:none after switchTab('backup')"; passed on `[chromium-disk]` (existing tabs). | 2026-05-26 | Bug Fix | Accept-cluster: idempotent no_op for already-absorbed removals (PR pending, follow-up to #63) | User reported "Accepted 84 of 115 changes, 31 failed" on a baseline cluster — every one of the 31 failures was a removal-event for a Windows per-user service (`AarSvc_<sessionid>`, `BcastDVRUserService_<sessionid>`, `CDPUserSvc_<sessionid>`, …) where the trailing session id changes on every login. **Root cause**: `accept_drift_entry` (correctly) refused to remove a key that wasn't in the baseline with `"key 'X' not in baseline -- can't remove what isn't there"`. But the **desired end-state was already achieved** for those 31 events — the key was already absent. Counting them as failures was technically correct but UX-wrong: a `rm -f`-style idempotent no-op is the right semantic. Same situation when an added/changed event's key has since vanished from the current snapshot (user already removed it, or Windows auto-cleaned). **Fix**: `accept_cluster_events` now returns a three-way breakdown `{accepted, no_op, failed}`. A removal whose key isn't in baseline → `no_op`. An added/changed event whose key isn't in the current snapshot → `no_op` (was a soft failure). `ok=True` when `failed==0` regardless of `no_op` count. JS message switched from `Accepted X of N, Y failed` to `✅ All N changes resolved. X written · Y already in desired state (no-op)` when nothing truly failed. **Reproduces the user's exact numbers**: `test_user_report_115_event_cluster_with_31_already_absorbed_removals` builds 34 real adds + 50 real removes + 31 already-absent removes = 115 events, asserts `accepted=84, no_op=31, failed=0, ok=True`. **+3 regression tests** in TestAcceptClusterEvents; existing `test_added_event_not_in_snapshot_records_soft_failure` retargeted as `test_added_event_not_in_snapshot_classified_as_no_op`. 2,550 → 2,553 tests, 86.61% → 86.62% coverage. `dev.py verify` all-green. |

User asked for a new pane that combines existing Quick Fixes with recurring scans for testing coverage, code bugs, security, and tech debt. **PR-1 ships**: new "🛠 Utilities" tab in the nav; Quick Fixes moved out of Dashboard (Dashboard now shows a deep-link card → Utilities); new `codehealth.py` (~390 lines) with 4 on-demand scanners — **coverage** reads existing `.coverage` via `coverage json -o -` (does NOT re-run pytest; that's PR-2's button since pytest --cov is ~48s), **ruff** runs `ruff check . --output-format=json` and categorises by prefix (S=critical, F=warning, UP/PIE=info), **secrets** runs `scripts/check_repo_secrets.py --all` (existing pre-commit scanner with built-in `<REDACTED>` invariant; counts `Finding:` markers), **tech_debt** pure file-walk counting TODO/FIXME/XXX/HACK markers + files ≥5,000 lines (skips `.git`/`__pycache__`/`Logs`/`.claude` etc.). Severity ladder `ok < info < warning < critical`; worst-wins rollup. **Recurring surface (PR-1)**: tray boot fires `maybe_run_on_boot()` which spawns a background thread if state is >7 days stale or never run. **Routes**: `GET /api/codehealth/status` (last result + `is_stale` + `is_running` + threshold), `POST /api/codehealth/run` (202 on kick-off, 409 if already running). **UI**: 4 cards in a grid (auto-fit minmax(240px,1fr)) with coloured left border by severity, last-run timestamp + duration, "▶ Scan now" button with auto-poll while in flight. **Live smoke against real repo**: coverage 86.6% (ok), ruff 12 findings (1 F + 1 PIE + 10 UP modernisations → warning), secrets clean, tech debt 4 TODOs + 4 files ≥5k lines (windesktopmgr.py at 12k is the worst offender) → worst=warning. **+37 tests** in TestStatePersistence ×7, TestScanCoverage ×5, TestScanRuff ×6, TestScanSecrets ×3, TestScanTechDebt ×5, TestWorstLevel ×4, TestBackgroundRunGuard ×3, TestCodeHealthRoutes ×4. 2,553 → 2,590 tests, 86.62% → ~86.6% coverage. architecture.html: +1 fs-chip (codehealth.py) + 1 test-chip (test_codehealth.py), 73 → 75 API endpoints, total badge bumped. TAB_IDS in test_playwright_smoke.py extended to include "utilities" (closes the gap PR #54 surfaced for "backup"). **PR-2 scope (deferred)**: configurable weekly cron + trend history (last N runs, sparkline per scanner) + a pytest --cov-running "Refresh coverage" button. |

| 2026-06-03 | Bug Fix | Cleared baseline drift still showed as open on dashboard (PR #99) | User: "i cleared off all drift but still showing as open." **Root cause:** the two dashboard drift concerns ("System baseline drift detected" + "Cross-surface drift cluster") read the rolling 24h drift HISTORY (`baseline.recent_drift()` + `baseline.load_history()`→`correlation_alert()`), NOT live drift. `accept_current_as_baseline()` only rewrote the baseline file; it never pruned history, so recorded events kept firing the concerns for up to 24h. Calling `compute_drift()` in the dashboard hot path was not viable (5-30s snapshot per refresh). **Fix — acceptance watermark:** new `ACCEPT_WATERMARK_FILE` + `_save_accept_watermark()`/`load_accept_watermark()`; `accept_current_as_baseline()` records the watermark (= accepted snapshot ts) on a successful baseline write; new `drop_accepted(entries)` filters history to entries STRICTLY after the watermark (fail-open on undateable entries — never hides drift we can't date; drops non-dict junk; unchanged when no watermark); `dashboard.py` wraps both `recent_drift()` and `load_history()` in `drop_accepted()`. Baseline tab's own history/timeline intentionally still shows the FULL audit trail (dashboard = live "open?" view; timeline = audit). Partial-accept paths (accept_drift_entry/accept_cluster_events) deliberately don't move the watermark (only "accept all" is the canonical reviewed-everything signal); they self-heal on the next background drift recording. **GAP TYPE: missing-state-reconciliation — a "cleared/closed" UI indicator was derived from raw append-only history instead of being gated on the acceptance action.** **REUSABLE LESSON: the prior accept ran under OLD code so no watermark was written — the fix is NOT retroactive; live-validated by re-running /api/baseline/accept (the user's intended action), confirming the watermark wrote {accepted_at} and drop_accepted collapsed 1 recent + 113 historical entries → 0/0, and the live dashboard baseline concerns went 2→0 after the 30s summary-cache TTL.** +14 tests (12 test_baseline TestAcceptWatermark + 2 test_routes dashboard suppression), 2,698 passed @ 87.18%. code-review (feature-dev:code-reviewer): 0 critical/high; resolved the best-effort watermark-write-failure test gap; justified the intentional timeline-vs-dashboard asymmetry. arch.html: badge 2,426→2,440, test_routes chip 268→270, test_baseline chip 165→176 + new `baseline_accept_watermark.json` cache chip; drift test green. Live-verified via dev.py verify (post_restart + topology passed). |

| 2026-06-04 | Bug Fix | `/api/baseline/drift` hung >90s — bounded the WMI Win32_Service COM call (PR #105) | User-reported (via spawned task): Baseline tab stuck on its loading spinner; raw `curl` to `/api/baseline/drift` timed out at 90s (no browser/CSS → pure backend). **Root cause:** `baseline._collect_services_wmi_enrichment()`'s `wmi.WMI()` + `Win32_Service()` COM enumeration HANGS indefinitely when Winmgmt is wedged — a hang is not an exception, so the existing try/except couldn't rescue it. Proven via a per-collector timing probe: the call hangs only when COM is initialized (the live Flask thread); a fresh thread without `CoInitialize` fast-fails (why standalone tests missed it). **Fix:** extracted `_wmi_service_enrichment_query()`; ran it in an abandonable daemon worker (CoInitializes itself) bounded by `_WMI_ENRICH_TIMEOUT_S=8s`; returns `{}` on timeout (psutil service data unaffected). **GAP TYPE: unbounded-blocking-call on a request hot path. PREVENTION: every COM/WMI/subprocess call on a request path must be bounded.** +2 tests; ruff clean; unit suite 86.7%; code-review 3 findings all resolved (timing-bound, dead-test assertion, CoInitialize/CoUninitialize balance). Live-verified: endpoint returned 200 in 13.2s (was 90s+ timeout) with valid payload. arch.html test badge 2,440→2,442 + test_baseline chip 176→178. |

| 2026-06-05 | Perf | Baseline snapshot parallelized + WMI cache/in-flight guard — keeps tab fast under degraded WMI (PR #106) | Follow-up to #105. The hang was bounded but the tab was still slow (~13s) and a SECOND unbounded path surfaced: `psutil.win_service_iter()` itself can stall tens of seconds on a degraded SCM. **Fix (baseline.py):** (1) `take_snapshot()` runs its 3 collectors CONCURRENTLY (wall time = slowest, not sum); (2) `_collect_services()` overlaps WMI enrichment with the psutil iteration; (3) `_collect_services_wmi_enrichment()` gains a short-TTL cache + an **IN-FLIGHT GUARD** (one worker at a time — while one is stuck on a wedged WMI, concurrent calls return cache/`{}` IMMEDIATELY, bounding the COM-thread leak to one AND keeping repeat snapshots fast) + worker-side cache publish + stale-serve; (4) `take_snapshot()` caps each collector at `_SNAPSHOT_COLLECTOR_TIMEOUT_S=45s` and RAISES on breach (not `{}` → no false "all removed" drift). **REUSABLE LESSON: an abandon-on-timeout thread bounds a hang but leaks one thread per call — pair it with an in-flight guard so a persistent wedge serves cache instead of piling up leaked threads; this is also what makes repeat calls fast (the tab re-snapshots on interaction).** **Live-validated on freshly-restarted tray: drift 8.5s cold / ~3.0s warm (was >90s hang); baseline Playwright smoke 6/6 PASS in isolation.** +5 tests; ruff clean; unit suite 86.7%; code-review 2 findings BOTH resolved (CRITICAL psutil-unbounded → budget+raise; IMPORTANT inflight-stuck-on-Thread.start-failure → guarded). `dev.py verify` green. arch.html test badge 2,442→2,447 + test_baseline chip 178→183. **ENV CAVEAT: the machine's WMI/SCM subsystem is degraded under sustained load (the full Playwright suite's OTHER WMI callers — drivers `_wmi_conn` etc. — re-wedge it, so baseline smoke fails in a FULL run but passes in isolation/after restart). Needs a REBOOT to fully recover. Spawned a follow-up to bound the remaining WMI call sites.** |

| 2026-06-07 | Bug Fix | Keep dev.py verify green after the AI identifier — CPU-budget false positive (PR #115) | Follow-up to #114. The global AI identifier made `dev.py verify` fail `post_restart_check`: the process/service/startup enrichment workers now add a Claude call to their lookup chain, so the post-selftest background drain runs longer and the CPU-budget sampler caught the tray MID-DRAIN at 98% (budget 25%). **Confirmed TRANSIENT, not a leak** — manual sampling showed the tray settles to ~0% within seconds once queues finish. **Three fixes: (1)** `ai_identify.py` reuses ONE lazily-built Anthropic client across the concurrent workers (was a fresh client + TLS handshake per call) → the lookup burst pools connections, CPU spike far smaller; added `identify_status()` + `GET /api/identify/status`. **(2)** `post_restart_check.py` tracks the new AI queue in the drain-wait + raised `QUEUE_DRAIN_TIMEOUT_S` 120→240 (early-exits on drain, no warm-cache cost). **(3)** the CPU check now RESAMPLES 3× and takes the MINIMUM — a real poll/refresh leak stays high on every sample, transient drain settles, so it only fails on a SUSTAINED reading. **REUSABLE LESSON: a "sustained CPU leak" gate must RESAMPLE (min across a window), not take one sample — otherwise any feature that does transient post-restart background work (enrichment, AI lookups, a dashboard fan-out) trips a false positive; and any new background queue must be added to `wait_for_background_queues`' endpoint list so the sampler waits for it.** Root-caused by direct psutil sampling of the live tray (98% during the check → 0% a minute later). +5 tests (identify_status fn + route; transient-spike-passes; sustained-high-cpu-fails; 3-sample assertion; endpoint-count-agnostic drain fixtures). 2,789 unit tests pass. code-review N/A (verify-infra fix, empirically validated). arch.html: endpoints 131→132. **FOLLOW-UP PR #116 (sample-until-idle):** #115's 3-sample-min was STILL flaky (passed once, failed once) — the CPU check's own `/api/dashboard/summary` probe kicks a single-flight 8-collector dashboard fan-out that runs ~6-15s at ~96% CPU on a cold restart, outlasting the fixed 6s window. Replaced the fixed N-sample window with **"sample until idle (2s windows) or fail after IDLE_CONFIRM_TIMEOUT_S=40s"** — passes the instant the tray idles (proof it CAN idle), fails only if it never settles (a real leak). **REUSABLE LESSON: a transient-vs-sustained CPU gate should WAIT FOR IDLE with a timeout, not sample a fixed window — you can't guess how long a legitimate cold-start burst (dashboard fan-out + AI drain + cold WMI) will take.** Verified GREEN twice back-to-back (74.9s, 74.2s). `dev.py verify` GREEN. |
| 2026-06-07 | Feature | Global "auto-identify unknown entities via AI" rule — PR1 (PR #114) | Motivated by `vmmem` showing "No description found. Search the process name online to identify it." — the app punted identification to the user. Now it looks it up ITSELF as a global rule. **Audit first (user asked to examine all parts):** 5 entity types (processes/services/startup/BSOD/events) ALREADY auto-look-up via a worker chain ending in a Microsoft Learn web search — but MS Learn has no `vmmem` article → fell through to the placeholder; the fix is a smarter last-resort engine, not new plumbing. 3 types (drivers/MAC/BIOS) had NO lookup. **New module `ai_identify.py` (~277 lines)** — named `ai_identify` NOT `identify` (a top-level `identify.py` SHADOWS the PyPI `identify` package that pre-commit imports → broke the commit hook; aliased `as identify` in callers). `identify_via_ai()` = ONE bounded Claude **haiku** call → `{plain, what, safe_kill}`, graceful `None` on no-key/SDK-missing/timeout/unparseable/cap; `identify()` = non-blocking cache-or-enqueue subsystem (daemon worker + single-flight + atomic JSON cache) for entities lacking their own worker. **Engine choice = Claude AI** (user picked, over broadened-web-search); reuses the `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` already configured for NLQ; sends ONLY the entity name + minimal context, never system telemetry; `MAX_AI_LOOKUPS` per-process cap. **Wiring:** processes/services/startup worker fallbacks (fixes the vmmem CLASS — `processes._resolve_process` extracted, unit-testable) + driver "Unknown Device" / BIOS empty-version / MAC IEEE-miss gap-fills. **Live-verified:** 13 processes resolved via `claude_ai` incl. vmmem → "Hyper-V Virtual Machine Memory … safe to stop if you're not using VMs"; cowork-svc honestly returns "I do not recognise this process" (graceful). **REUSABLE LESSONS: (1) never name a top-level module after a build-tool dependency (`identify` collides with pre-commit's pkg). (2) Cap COSTLY operations on SUCCESS only — failed AI calls must refund the cap or a network blip permanently disables the feature. (3) Don't PERSIST negative/"unresolved" lookups — a first run before the API key is set would cache-poison every entity forever; keep them in-memory (session dedupe) and let a restart retry clean.** +38 tests (test_identify.py 28: engine happy/no-key/no-sdk/api-error/unparseable/cap + failed-calls-refund + prose-prefix-JSON; subsystem cache/single-flight/pending; unresolved-NOT-persisted; test_identify_integration.py 10: process AI fallback + driver/bios/mac wiring + skip-when-known). AI fully mocked. ruff clean; unit suite 86.6%. code-review (feature-dev:code-reviewer) **3 IMPORTANT ALL RESOLVED** (cap burned by failed calls; unresolved persisted to disk; _extract_json prose-prefix brace dropped valid JSON); thread-safety + non-blocking-on-request-threads + no-retry-storm reviewed clean. arch.html: badge 2,733→2,769, new ai_identify.py chip + module-row entry, registered test_identify*.py in drift TEST_FILES. `dev.py verify` green. **PR1 of phased rollout (engine + process-class fix + 3 gap entities); events/BSOD migration onto the shared engine + the deferred LHM auto-start ("option 2") are the queued follow-ups.** |
