| 2026-06-08 | Bug Fix | Dashboard gauges: replace radial rings with horizontal bars to kill the "dark/black pre-filled section" (PR #127) | **End of the #122→#126 ring saga.** The user reported, across FIVE PRs (dark `--border` track → faint white .07 → dim-accent → fully transparent/no track), that the gauge STILL showed a dark "pre-filled" section. **Final diagnosis (confirmed live):** it is inherent to the *ring geometry*, not the track colour — a thin colour arc on the near-black card (`#111623`) always leaves the rest of the ring's 270° circular path reading as dark. Proven with two live Playwright probes: a magenta-tile test showed the unfilled ring is transparent and shows the CARD through it; a hide-arc test showed the ring IS the arc (no separate dark element). No track variant can fix a curved remainder on a dark card. **Fix:** abandon the circular geometry — each gauge is now a **horizontal bar**: label, big colour-coded number (`--gcol`), and a colour fill (`--col`, width = value%) on a small light track (`rgba(255,255,255,.07)`). A straight bar's track is unambiguous UI chrome with no large curved remainder, so nothing can read as a dark filled section. **css:** dropped `.dg-ring`/`.dg-arc`/`.dg-core`; added `.dg-bar`/`.dg-fill`; `.dg-unavail` moved to the `.db-gauge` tile; `.dg-num` coloured by `--gcol`; `--sweep` still set on the element that READS it (`.dg-fill`) since `@property --sweep` is `inherits:false` (the #122 lesson); defensive `var(--col, var(--cyan))`/`var(--glow, …)` fallbacks so an unresolved colour can't make the fill inherit the track; `animation:none` on unavailable fills. **js** `renderGauges()`: emit `.dg-bar > .dg-fill`; set `--gcol/--col/--glow` on the tile; tile gets `dg-unavail` when value null. code-review (feature-dev:code-reviewer) **no critical findings; 3 IMPORTANT all applied** (var() fallbacks, animation:none on unavailable, and a proportionality assertion replacing the fragile "fills must all differ" check that could false-fail on coincidentally-equal sensors). Playwright gauge regression rewritten: asserts each bar fill is PROPORTIONAL to its value AND no `.dg-ring`/`.dg-arc` geometry remains. Verified live on the deployed tray: bars fill 35.7/39/23/73.8, CPU-temp degrades to "—"/NO SENSOR, **dark section gone** (screenshot sent). 2,834+ unit tests pass; gauge + thermals Playwright pass; `dev.py verify` GREEN. **REUSABLE LESSON: when a user repeatedly reports a "dark section" on a thin coloured ARC over a dark card, stop iterating the track — the dark is the card showing through the ring's curved remainder. A horizontal bar (small straight light track + proportional fill) has no such remainder. Don't iterate geometry-bound cosmetics five times; diagnose the geometry.** |
| 2026-06-08 | Feature | Auto-reload the page when a deploy changes the front-end bundle (PR #124) | **Root-caused the recurring "I shipped a CSS/JS fix but you still see the old version"** (it hit PRs #121/#122/#123 — the user kept seeing stale gauges): the open page's 3s heartbeat polls `/api/health` and auto-refreshes DATA, but NEVER re-fetches the stylesheet/script, so after a deploy (tray restart) the browser keeps running the assets it loaded at page-open until a manual reload. `Cache-Control: no-cache` makes a *reload* fetch fresh, but nothing triggers the reload. **Fix:** `_assets_version()` = 12-char sha256 of the mtimes of app.css/app.js/index.html, returned by `/api/health` as `assets` (mtime-keyed module cache so the digest isn't recomputed every poll; OSError-guarded → never raises); the frontend heartbeat records the first token it sees and `window.location.reload()`s once when a later poll reports a different token (a deploy). No loop (the reloaded page re-baselines against the now-stable post-deploy version); the version-change `return`s before the reconnect-banner block so it composes with the restart heartbeat-recovery. **REUSABLE LESSON: a SPA-style dashboard that polls for data must ALSO detect front-end-bundle changes and reload — `no-cache` only helps on a reload that never happens on its own. Expose an asset-version on the heartbeat and reload on change.** Verified live end-to-end: touched an asset → the open page reloaded itself within one heartbeat (a `window.__reloadMarker` was cleared), re-baselining to the new version. code-review (feature-dev:code-reviewer) **1 IMPORTANT RESOLVED** (cache the digest on the `/api/health` hot path — polled every 3s/client + by `dev.py verify`); reload-loop safety, malformed-data handling, reconnect-path interaction, mtime-as-deploy-signal all clean. +5 tests (health includes assets; version stable-then-changes-on-mtime; graceful-when-files-missing). 2,834 unit tests pass. arch badge 2,820→2,823. `dev.py verify` GREEN. **NOTE: helps from the NEXT deploy on — a page open before this shipped still needs one manual reload.** |
| 2026-06-08 | Bug Fix | Dashboard gauge ring track was dark/"black" — faint groove (PR #123) | Follow-up to #122: user flagged the gauge still had a black section. The radial gauge's unfilled remainder (the 270° arc past the value) was painted in `--border` (#1c2535) — a near-black that read as a "black pre-filled" section behind the coloured fill. Found by inspecting the live conic-gradient (computed `backgroundImage` = `...rgb(28,37,53) 20%..75%...`). Changed the conic track segment from `var(--border)` to `rgba(255,255,255,.07)` — a subtle groove that reads as "empty" so only the value fill draws the eye; transparent 90° bottom gap + mask/donut unchanged. Trivial 1-line cosmetic change (reviewed inline — no logic/security surface; `/code-review` agent skipped as disproportionate per judgement, documented). Verified live: dark arc gone, clean coloured fill on a faint track. Extended the #122 gauge regression to assert the track is NOT the dark `--border` (guards the black-section returning). 2,831 unit tests pass. `dev.py verify` GREEN. **LESSON: an unfilled gauge/progress track should be a faint translucent groove (rgba white ~.07), not a solid theme-`--border` colour, which on a dark card reads as a filled black arc.** |
| 2026-06-08 | Bug Fix | Dashboard gauge rings never filled + colour-coded tile accents (PR #122) | User: "expand colour coding to all dashboard panels" + "the usage in the panels is all different but the space used by the charts is the same." **Diagnosed the second as a real latent bug (since PR2 #110):** the radial-gauge coloured arc NEVER filled — every ring rendered identical/empty regardless of value. Root cause found by inspecting computed styles live: `--sweep` is a registered `@property{inherits:false}` (registered so it animates smoothly), but `renderGauges` set it on `.dg-ring` while the conic-gradient + `dg-sweep` animation that READ it live on the child `.dg-arc`; with `inherits:false` the arc never received the value and stayed at the `initial-value:0` → 0% fill on all gauges. **Fix:** set `--sweep` on the `.dg-arc` element that reads + animates it (not the parent ring). Rings now fill 0→value (verified live: cpu_load 35.8, cpu_temp 45, gpu_util 18, memory 75.4 — each a distinct arc). **Enhancement (the colour-coding ask):** each gauge tile gets a state-coloured top accent — `renderGauges` sets `--gcol`=the gauge's threshold colour, CSS `border-top:3px solid var(--gcol)`; whole tile reads health at a glance (memory 75%→orange, load→green, temps→cyan), matching the colour-banded thermal panels. Banner + concern cards were already level-coloured. **REUSABLE LESSON: a registered `@property{inherits:false}` does NOT cascade to children — set it on the exact element that READS it (the conic/animation), not a convenient parent. When a "chart" looks identical across different values, check whether the fill variable is actually reaching the element that renders the fill (computed-style inspection in the browser pins it fast).** code-review (feature-dev:code-reviewer) **CLEAN — no findings** (animation still 0→value + holds via `both` fill; `--col`/`--glow` still inherit to the arc as unregistered props; unavailable gauge muted-accent/empty-arc/"—" unchanged; Thermals hero row inherits the same fix; sweep clamped 0-100). +1 Playwright regression (arc `--sweep` set per-value ON the .dg-arc + fills actually DIFFER across gauges + tile accent present; reads the inline target not the mid-animation computed value to avoid timing flake). 2,831 unit tests pass. arch badge 2,819→2,820. `dev.py verify` GREEN. |
| 2026-06-08 | UI/Frontend | Temperature colour-coding for the Thermals panels + legend (PR #121) | User asked for colour coding on the thermal panels. The per-core grid rendered every cell the same cyan at idle (old `tempColor` only differentiated at 65°C+), so you couldn't tell hot cores from cool at a glance. **Frontend-only:** replaced inline `tempColor()` with `tempBand(c)` → a band name (cool < 50, normal 50-69, warm 70-84, hot 85-94, crit ≥ 95); per-core cells + sensor rows get `tband-<band>`; a `#th-legend` maps each colour to its range. **app.css:** each `.tband-*` sets `--tband`(accent)+`--tband-bg`(faint fill) custom props which the cell/row left-accent + tint + readout-colour + bar-fill + legend-swatch all read via inheritance; `.tband-crit` pulses (reduced-motion guarded). cool=cyan, normal=green, warm=amber, hot=orange, crit=red. DOM-built (no innerHTML). **REUSABLE LESSON: drive multi-element colour-coding from ONE band function + a `--tband` CSS custom property set on the container — children inherit it, so the JS just adds a class and the CSS handles accent/readout/bar/legend consistently (no per-element inline colours to keep in sync).** Verified live: 24 cores cyan at ~35°C idle, legend shows all 5 bands, the sensor list renders a real cool→normal→warm→hot gradient (per-sensor accent + number track the temp). code-review (feature-dev:code-reviewer) **CLEAN — no findings** (no dead tempColor refs, legend rebuilt without leak, CSS specificity + `--tband` inheritance correct, tempBand NaN/negative-safe, sensor bar width preserved). +1 Playwright (cells banded + legend 5 bands + accent colour ACTUALLY tracks the band, not just "a class exists" — per the visual-smoke-isn't-correctness memo). 2,831 unit tests pass. arch badge 2,818→2,819. `dev.py verify` GREEN. |
| 2026-06-08 | Feature | Logs tab exact-level filtering — "INFO only" (PR #120) | User reported the log filter "shows warnings when I choose INFO." **Diagnosed as NOT a bug:** the dropdown's `INFO+` means "minimum severity" (info AND above — WARNING/ERROR are MORE severe), the industry-standard convention (matches Python logging; backend param is literally `min_level`). The "+" + the fact that DEBUG was correctly excluded proved the filter worked. But the user's expectation (see only INFO) is reasonable → offered it as a choice (AskUserQuestion → "add exact-level filtering"). **Implementation:** dropdown now has two optgroups — "Minimum severity (and above)" DEBUG+/INFO+/WARNING+/ERROR+/CRITICAL+ and "This level only" =DEBUG…=CRITICAL. `applogging.read_recent(exact=False)` keeps only the exact level when set (still scans the full file so a rare exact-WARNING isn't buried in the INFO-flooded tail); `_parse_log_query` returns `exact` from `?exact=1`; both `/api/logs` + `/api/logs/download` pass it; `app.js logParseLevel()` decodes the dropdown's leading `=` into `{level, exact}` → `=INFO`→`level=INFO&exact=1`. Verified live: INFO only→1752 INFO/0 warnings; WARNING only→13; INFO+ still all three. **REUSABLE LESSON: "minimum severity" log filtering (selected-level-and-above) is the convention and usually correct (you don't want to miss warnings while watching info) — when a user calls it a bug, explain the semantics + offer exact filtering rather than silently changing the default.** code-review (feature-dev:code-reviewer) **2 IMPORTANT both RESOLVED: (1) `?level=BOGUS` ranked as DEBUG → silently dumped the WHOLE file; now validated at the route boundary (unknown→all-levels tail, `_VALID_LEVELS` enforced) +2 tests. (2) CRITICAL+ missing from the min-severity group (asymmetric with the exact group) → added.** +9 tests (exact info/warning, min-vs-exact differ, exact passthrough on both routes, bogus-level fallback, level-uppercased; 2 Playwright: dropdown has both modes + parser decodes, exact INFO returns info-only live). 2,831 unit tests pass. arch.html: badge 2,809→2,818, test_logging.py chip 24→33. `dev.py verify` GREEN. |
| 2026-06-08 | Bug Fix | LHM per-core temps were always blank — read its HTTP server, not WMI (PR #119) | User reported "installed LHM but CPU temp panel shows no data." **Root-caused by diagnosing the LIVE machine** (not assumptions): LHM was running ELEVATED (2 instances, verified via TokenElevation), but `root\LibreHardwareMonitor` WMI namespace didn't exist. Grepped the actual LHM binary → **NO `wmiMenuItem`/`WmiProvider`/`root\*HardwareMonitor` strings: LibreHardwareMonitor v0.9.6 has NO WMI provider at all** (unlike OpenHardwareMonitor, which it forked from). Found `runWebServerMenuItem`/`ListenerPort`/`data.json`/`HttpListener` strings → LHM exposes sensors ONLY via its built-in HTTP server (`http://localhost:8085/data.json`), which ships OFF. So the entire LHM→Thermals integration (PRs #112/#113/#118) was built on a wrong assumption and never actually worked. **The whole feature class (install/launch/autostart) was reading a namespace that can never exist.** **Fix:** `lhm.get_lhm_temps()` reads data.json over loopback (requests, 3s bound, graceful [] on every failure, depth-capped recursive walk so a hostile payload can't RecursionError into the `/api/thermals/lhm/status` route, EXCLUDES "Distance to TjMax" headroom sensors that LHM mistypes as Temperature → were doubling the per-core grid + poisoning the gauge with a ~66°C idle reading); `is_running()` probes the web server; `_ensure_config()` enables the web server (`runWebServerMenuItem=true`,`ListenerPort=8085`) at install + before each launch to migrate old installs, **only writing when the keys are missing so it never clobbers settings the user set in LHM's own UI**; `thermals.get_thermals()` merges the web temps (dropping the coarse WMI zone), kept the OHM WMI path; `dashboard._build_gauges` cpu_temp now picks the hottest CPU sensor by SensorId (`/intelcpu/`) instead of max-of-ALL (a hot GPU/NVMe temp was masquerading as CPU temp once 91 sensors merged in). **Security:** user authorized (AskUserQuestion) enabling the local web server; LHM binds all interfaces but Windows Firewall default-deny inbound (verified on, no allow rule) keeps it loopback-only — documented. **REUSABLE LESSONS: (1) NEVER assume a forked tool kept its predecessor's integration surface — verify against the ACTUAL binary (LHM dropped OHM's WMI provider). (2) Diagnose the LIVE system before theorizing (process elevation check + WMI namespace enum + binary string grep pinned it in minutes). (3) When merging a foreign sensor tree, a vendor can mistype sensors (Distance-to-TjMax as Temperature) — filter, and don't `max()` across heterogeneous sources for a specific gauge.** Verified LIVE: 91 temps, 24 per-core (P-Core #1-8 + E-Core #1-16 of the i9-14900K), CPU-temp gauge 37-39°C, install CTA correctly gone. +14 tests (web-server parse/timeout/deep-payload/non-dict; is_running web; ensure_config preserve-vs-migrate; thermals merge; cpu-sensor-filter gauge). 2,824 unit tests pass. code-review (feature-dev:code-reviewer) **4 IMPORTANT ALL RESOLVED** (stale WMI JS comment; `_walk` no depth cap → RecursionError into a live route; `_ensure_config` overwriting user config every launch; missing requests.Timeout test). arch.html: badge 2,795→2,809, lhm.py + thermals.py chips corrected (WMI→HTTP). `dev.py verify` GREEN. |
| 2026-06-08 | Feature | Optional LHM auto-start at login — the deferred "option 2" (PR #118) | Closes the LHM auto-start the user deferred during the #113 installer build. Adds a toggle on the Thermals LHM CTA that registers an at-logon scheduled task launching LibreHardwareMonitor ELEVATED, so the per-core grid + CPU-temp gauge stay populated across reboots without re-clicking "Launch as admin". **lhm.py (~340 lines now):** `autostart_status()` (unelevated `schtasks /Query`), `setup_autostart()` (`schtasks /Create … /SC ONLOGON /RL HIGHEST /F` via `ShellExecuteW("runas")` UAC), `remove_autostart()` (`/Delete /F` elevated), shared `_schtasks_elevated()` helper. Fixed `AUTOSTART_TASK_NAME` constant; only other value is the verified exe path. ShellExecuteW returns on LAUNCH (not schtasks' exit code) so the UI re-queries status to confirm. **3 routes:** GET/POST `/api/thermals/lhm/autostart` + POST `…/autostart/remove`. **app.js:** `thRenderLhmAutostart()` lazily fetches status + renders a Set-up/turn-off toggle (DOM methods + textContent, no innerHTML). code-review (feature-dev:code-reviewer) **1 CRITICAL + 2 IMPORTANT, all resolved/justified: C1 (privilege escalation) — the task runs the `%LOCALAPPDATA%` exe elevated and that dir is USER-WRITABLE → a general user→admin escalation path; JUSTIFIED + documented in `setup_autostart` as an accepted trade-off for a single-user personal desktop (an attacker who can write `%LOCALAPPDATA%` as you already owns your account via per-user Run keys / their own logon tasks, so marginal risk is low; a `%ProgramFiles%`/ACL-locked install would break the no-admin install + one-click update; hardening to a non-user-writable dir is the documented upgrade path).** I2 (arg injection) — exe path rejects a stray double-quote before the `/TR` arg (checked before isfile so it's reachable; NTFS forbids quotes anyway) +test. I1 — added the missing remove-400 route test. **REUSABLE LESSON: registering a `/RL HIGHEST` logon task against a user-writable exe is a textbook escalation vector — if you accept it, DOCUMENT the threat-model justification in code + surface it to the user, don't ship it silently.** +13 tests (TestAutostart 8 incl. quote-guard + UAC-declined; 5 routes; 1 Playwright toggle-renders-when-installed, forcing `_lhmStatus.installed` — note it's a lexical top-level `let`, NOT on `window`, so the smoke test assigns the binding directly). Verified live: status endpoint enabled:false; toggle renders "⤴ Set up auto-start at login" beside "⚡ Launch as admin" when installed. 2,810 unit tests pass. arch.html: badge 2,781→2,795, endpoints 132→135, lhm.py chip updated. `dev.py verify` GREEN. **🎉 Both this session's queued follow-ups now shipped (identify rollout PR2 #117 + LHM auto-start #118).** |
| 2026-06-08 | Feature | AI identifier rollout PR2 — BSOD + Event-ID lookups onto the shared engine (PR #117) | Completes the global AI-identifier rollout (PR1 #114 = engine + processes/services/startup + driver/MAC/BIOS gaps). Adds the same AI fallback to the last two self-learning subsystems whose chains ended at a Microsoft Learn web search + "no description found" placeholder. **bsod.py:** extracted `_resolve_stop_code(code_norm)` from the worker loop; AI identifier inserted between MS-Learn web + placeholder, mapping `{plain, what}`→ BSOD `{source, title, detail, priority, action, fetched}` — resolves rare/vendor STOP codes MS Learn doesn't document. **events.py:** extracted `_resolve_event(event_id, source)`; AI fallback mapping → `{source, title, detail, noise, action, fetched}` — names third-party/obscure event IDs the provider registry + MS Learn miss. Both workers now delegate to the extracted (unit-testable) resolver. code-review (feature-dev:code-reviewer): schema mapping + thread-confinement (AI only on the background worker, never a request thread) clean; **1 CRITICAL found + FIXED — a PRE-EXISTING double `task_done()` on the cache-hit path in BOTH workers** (the `finally` already does in_flight-discard + task_done; the cache-hit early-exit did them INLINE too → double-counted task_done, would hang/raise a `queue.join()`). Removed the inline pair; `finally` owns them. **REUSABLE LESSON: when a worker's `finally` owns queue bookkeeping (discard + task_done), an early-`continue` branch must NOT repeat it — `continue` still runs `finally`.** +8 tests (bsod/event AI-resolves + placeholder + skip-when-known; 2 cache-hit→exactly-one-task_done regressions driven via SystemExit to stop the infinite worker loop after one iteration). 2,797 unit tests pass. arch.html: badge 2,769→2,781, ai_identify.py chip updated (now lists BSOD + Event-ID). `dev.py verify` GREEN. **🎉 Global identify rollout COMPLETE — all 7 punt-to-user entity types (process/service/startup/driver/MAC/BIOS/BSOD/event) now auto-identify.** |
| 2026-06-16 | Feature | In-app scheduled File History auto-cleanup — rolling retention (PR #131) | User wanted File History to keep only ~6 months of versions. **Research-first (web docs):** File History's NATIVE age-based retention only prunes when the target drive is FULL or on a manual cleanup (so it never enforces a rolling window / frees space on its own), and setting it requires the **deprecated** `IFhConfigMgr` COM API (CLSID `{ED43BB3C-…2177244C6DB4}`, vtable-only → needs comtypes). Surfaced these findings + the "doesn't proactively delete" caveat to the user (AskUserQuestion); they chose the **scheduled auto-cleanup** over the native-policy path. **Implementation:** a recurring `schtasks` task runs `fhmanagew -cleanup <N> -quiet` WEEKLY (deletes versions older than N days each run — actually prunes). `backup.py`: `fh_cleanup_schedule_status()` (unelevated `schtasks /Query /V /FO LIST` + regex parse of days/schedule, never raises), `setup_fh_cleanup_schedule(days)` (validated days → `/Create … /SC WEEKLY /ST 03:00 /RL HIGHEST /F`, UAC via `ShellExecuteW "runas"`, mirrors `lhm._schtasks_elevated`), `remove_fh_cleanup_schedule()`, `_fhmanagew_path()` → `%SYSTEMROOT%\System32\fhmanagew.exe`. **Security: the target is a NON-user-writable system binary, so the `/RL HIGHEST` task carries no escalation risk** (unlike the lhm autostart task's documented user-writable-exe trade-off); nothing user-controlled reaches the command line (fixed task name + path, range-validated int days, quote-refused path). 3 routes; DOM-built JS control (no innerHTML) in the Backup tab. code-review (feature-dev:code-reviewer) **0 critical, 2 resolved:** (1) `{"days": true}` POST coerced `int(True)==1` BEFORE the validator's bool guard → silently scheduled "-cleanup 1" (delete everything older than 1 day); added explicit bool reject in both schedule routes + the adjacent existing cleanup route (+regression test); (2) stale `validate_fh_cleanup_request` docstring (said cap 365; actual 3650). +24 tests; test_backup.py 112→126; arch badge 2,849→2,863, endpoints 135→138, 9→12 backup routes. Full suite green (86.8%); Playwright `TestCleanupScheduleControl` passes live. **NOTE: this does NOT change File History's native "Retention" row (stays NO LIMIT) — it's an independent app-managed prune; the UI explains this.** **REUSABLE LESSONS: (1) before building against a vendor setting, verify it actually does what the user wants — File History's "retention" doesn't proactively delete, so a scheduled cleanup is the right tool. (2) `int(True)==1` defeats a downstream `isinstance(bool)` guard if you coerce at the route boundary first — reject bool BEFORE `int()`. (3) a `/RL HIGHEST` scheduled task is only an escalation risk when its target is user-writable; a system32 binary is safe.** Commit 5076320. |
| 2026-06-16 | Bug Fix | File History cleanup gave no feedback + retention confusion (PR #130) | User ran a File History cleanup, approved the UAC/python prompt, but "couldn't tell if anything happened"; also the Retention row still said "NO LIMIT · keep ≥12 mo" after they set 6 months. **Diagnosed from the live audit log** (`backup_actions_history.json`): the cleanup ran fine — `fhmanagew -cleanup 180 -quiet` → exit 1, empty stderr = **nothing old enough to delete** (their oldest version is ~3 weeks). Two real bugs: (1) the elevated helper returned a bland `{"ok": True}` with no human detail, and `-quiet` prints nothing → banner just said "completed"; (2) **the result banner was wiped within ~1s** — all three backup actions (scan/cleanup/delete) call `bk_showActionResult()` then `loadBackup()`, and `loadBackup()` re-renders the SAME `#bk-overall` element with the health summary. **Fixes:** `_action_fh_cleanup` now returns `removed`+`summary` (rc==0 → "any versions that old removed" — worded to not over-claim since fhmanagew exit codes are undocumented; rc≠0+empty-stderr → "nothing to remove — and this is a one-time cleanup, not a retention change"; rc≠0+stderr → error). `bk_showActionResult` renders `result.summary` (escaped); `loadBackup(preserveBanner)` skips the `#bk-overall` overwrite; the 3 actions pass `loadBackup(true)` so the outcome persists while bodies refresh. **Retention (#2) is NOT an app bug:** `Config1.xml` AND `Config2.xml` both literally still read `NO LIMIT` / `MinimumRetentionAge 12` — File History never persisted the user's 6-month change (likely set in the wrong place / not saved, or set in Win11 "Windows Backup" which is a separate OneDrive system). The app reports the stored config faithfully. Added UI notes: Retention row says "(File History's saved policy — change in Control Panel → Advanced settings)"; cleanup note says it does NOT change retention. code-review (feature-dev:code-reviewer) **0 critical, 1 important RESOLVED** (softened rc==0 "removed" wording — undocumented exit codes), 1 style nit justified (per-test helper re-exec is harmless). +4 helper tests; test_backup.py 108→112; arch badge 2,845→2,849. Full suite green (86.7%); served JS verified live. **REUSABLE LESSONS: (1) an action that reports success must say WHAT it did, not just "completed" — especially when the underlying tool is `-quiet`/silent. (2) A transient result banner must not share a DOM node with a poller/refresh that overwrites it. (3) "Cleanup old versions" (one-time delete) ≠ "retention policy" (ongoing rule) — surface the distinction so users don't conflate them.** Commit 2d5c37b. |
| 2026-06-16 | Bug Fix | File History card cried "store missing" against a HEALTHY backup (PR #129) | User saw "Backup store on target: missing (higs7\SHIGS78-PC24\Data)" + "verify manually" on the Backup tab. **Live diagnosis (diagnose, don't theorize):** backups ARE current (catalog written minutes ago; `fhsvc` running; hourly cycles in the `FileHistory-Core/WHC` event log) — the configured store `E:\higs7\SHIGS78-PC24\Data` doesn't exist on disk (real store is `E:\SHIGS78-PC24\Data`, no `higs7\` user segment, ACL-restricted). **Genuine bug found:** `get_file_history_state` did `os.path.join(target_url.rstrip("\\/"), store_path)` — for drive-root `E:\` the rstrip → `E:`, and `os.path.join("E:", "higs7\\...")` yields the DRIVE-RELATIVE `E:higs7\...` (no separator), not absolute `E:\higs7\...`. **Fixes:** `_join_store_path` (absolute join + bare-drive guard); `_deepest_existing_ancestor` (honest probe message: "deepest existing ancestor: E:\" not a guessed "ACL"); reworked verdict so a FRESH catalog (≤7d, updated each protection cycle) is the authoritative "backups are current" signal → confident `ok` "File History is healthy — last backup activity N days ago" (ACL caveat when store unconfirmed), retiring the worried "info … verify manually". Frontend: store row shows calm muted "not readable from tray (ACL-protected — normal)" for non-critical verdicts, not red "missing". **KEY PIVOT (live discovery):** first tried the registry `ProtectedUpToTime` as the authority, but File History ZEROES it between hourly cycles (real value at 20:54, `0` by 21:xx) — too volatile; pivoted to catalog mtime. code-review (feature-dev:code-reviewer) **0 critical, 2 IMPORTANT both resolved:** (1) the catalog-fresh `ok` short-circuit swallowed the staging-area warning within the 7-day window → staging-full now still warns (checked first, +regression); (2) `_deepest_existing_ancestor` split on `\\` only → forward-slash paths truncated the message → normalize separators (+test). **REUSABLE LESSONS: (1) drive-root paths must be joined ABSOLUTELY — never `os.path.join` a rstripped `"E:"` (it's drive-relative). (2) A probe-based health signal that can false-negative (ACL / config-vs-disk path mismatch) must be backstopped by an independent "is it actually working" signal (catalog freshness) before it's allowed to alarm. (3) Verify a candidate "authoritative" signal is STABLE before trusting it — `ProtectedUpToTime` looked authoritative but is zeroed between cycles.** +23 tests; test_backup.py 85→108; arch badge 2,823→2,845. Live-verified on deployed tray: card now reads `ok` "File History is healthy — last backup activity 0.0 days ago". Commit 226cbf2. |
| 2026-06-17 | UI/Frontend | Specific, component-appropriate hot-temp advice (PR #136) | Follow-up to #135 (user: the alert "doesn't really help me figure out what I need to do"). The old elevated/critical-temp message was generic AND sometimes wrong: "Elevated temperatures: <list> → Monitor under load. Consider reapplying thermal paste if temps persist" — you can''t reapply thermal paste to an NVMe drive or a DIMM (airflow problems), and it never named WHICH component or HOW hot vs the limit. **Fix:** `summarize_thermals` now names the hottest component, the overage, and gives per-part advice via two new `thermals.py` helpers: `_component_category(name, sensor_id)` buckets by LHM SensorId (`/gpu` `/nvme` `/ram` `/intelcpu` `/lpc`) with a name fallback for WMI readings; `_hot_temp_insight(level, hot, threshold)` builds the text + advice. Examples: "CRITICAL CPU temperature: CPU Package at 98°C (3°C over the 95°C critical line). Also: P-Core #3 96°C." → clean heatsink/check Processes/reseat+paste; "Elevated storage drive temperature: Composite Temperature at 83°C (3°C over the 80°C warning line)." → NVMe writes usually transient, add M.2 heatsink/airflow (NO paste); memory → "airflow-related, not paste"; GPU → clean heatsink/fan-curve. Status/headline logic unchanged. code-review **justified-skip** (message/advice refactor, thorough unit tests, no new external-input path or security/data-flow surface — pure string + dict-lookup). +11 tests (TestComponentCategory ×7 buckets incl. name-only fallback + unknown→system; critical names component+overage+lists others; warning storage advice has NO "thermal paste"; warning leads with hottest). test count 2,868→2,879; arch badge updated. **REUSABLE LESSON: a health alert must say WHICH thing + HOW FAR over the line + what to actually DO for THAT component — generic "monitor it / reapply paste" advice is noise (and wrong for non-CPU/GPU parts). Bucket by sensor source/id and tailor the remedy.** Commit 236c5a4. |
| 2026-06-17 | Bug Fix | False "elevated/critical temperature" alert — LHM threshold registers flagged as live temps (PR #135) | User got a dashboard temp alert listing "Thermal Sensor Critical High Limit 85°C", "Warning Temperature 81°C", "Critical Temperature 84°C" while EVERY real sensor was 32-55°C. **Root cause (confirmed on live data):** LibreHardwareMonitor exposes SSD/DIMM **threshold + metadata registers** as `Type=="Temperature"` sensors — NVMe `Warning Temperature`/`Critical Temperature` (static SMART trip-points 81/84), DIMM `Thermal Sensor [Critical] [High/Low] Limit` (55/85), `Temperature Sensor Resolution` (0.3 metadata) — and `summarize_thermals` compared those STATIC values against the warn/crit bands. Same class as the PR #119 "Distance to TjMax" filter. **Fix:** extended the single TjMax exclusion in `lhm.get_lhm_temps()` into `_is_reading_temp(name)` rejecting `_NON_READING_TEMP_MARKERS = ("distance to tjmax","high limit","low limit","resolution","warning temperature","critical temperature")` (case-insensitive substrings). Filtering at the LHM SOURCE fixes the per-core grid, the hottest-sensor gauge, AND every summarizer/dashboard consumer at once. Live-verified: temps 67→45 (22 pseudo-sensors gone), 0 flagged, summary status `ok`, dashboard temp concern CLEARED. code-review (feature-dev:code-reviewer) **0 critical, 1 important RESOLVED:** original `"limit"` marker too broad (could drop a real reading containing "limit") → narrowed to two-word `"high limit"`/`"low limit"` suffixes (+boundary test: "Power Limit Temperature"/"CPU Temp Limit Zone" KEPT); 1 pre-existing note JUSTIFIED (`is_running()` false if every sensor filtered — theoretical, every real machine reports CPU core temps). +6 tests; test_lhm.py 49→50; arch badge 2,863→2,868 + lhm.py chip. **REUSABLE LESSON: a third-party sensor source (LHM) mistypes thresholds/limits/metadata as live readings — filter pseudo-sensors at the SOURCE by name BEFORE they reach health-threshold comparisons; never let a sensor's static trip-point value alert as if it were the reading. (Second instance of this exact LHM bug class after #119 TjMax — the marker list is the place to add the next one.)** Commit 011ea88. |
| 2026-06-17 | Bug Fix | Backup action feedback rendered off-screen — cleanup "looked like a no-op" (PR #134) | Follow-up to #133: user ran the cleanup and still "didn''t see it." **Root cause (different from #133):** the action feedback banner `#bk-overall` renders at the TOP of the Backup tab, but the Cleanup button sits near the BOTTOM of the File History section — so the progress + result appeared off-screen above the viewport; combined with the ~2s "nothing to remove" runtime, clicking the button read as silence. **Fix:** `overall.scrollIntoView({block:"center"})` when the cleanup progress banner appears AND inside `bk_showActionResult` (so every action result — cleanup, schedule setup/remove, scan, delete — is brought into view, never an off-screen no-op). Also hardened the pre-existing (#131) `TestCleanupScheduleControl` wait budget 6s→15s: the schedule-status route shells out to `schtasks /Query` (~2.2s warm, more cold), which flaked the tight wait on a cold-restart tray. code-review **justified-skip** (~6 LOC JS: two scrollIntoView calls + a test timeout bump, no new sink/route/fetch). Both backup-tab Playwright smokes pass live; served bundle carries 3 scrollIntoView sites. arch.html ⏭️ no trigger. **REUSABLE LESSON: feedback for an action must render WHERE THE USER IS LOOKING — a top-of-page banner for a bottom-of-page button reads as "nothing happened" even when it worked; scroll it into view. (PR #133 fixed the frozen-banner half; this fixed the off-screen half — two distinct causes of the same "I don''t see it" report.)** Commit acf1e86. |
| 2026-06-17 | Bug Fix | File History cleanup showed no live progress — "no way to see if it''s doing anything" (PR #133) | User report on the one-time "Cleanup old versions" button. **Root cause:** `bk_fhCleanup` called `bk_pollUntilDone(session_id)` WITHOUT the optional `statusEl` arg, so the poller''s per-tick "Running... Ns elapsed" updates had nowhere to render — the banner froze at a static "Running fhmanagew..." line until the result landed, and since `fhmanagew -quiet` emits no output, the run looked dead. **Fix:** build the progress banner with DOM methods (createElement/textContent) instead of innerHTML so we can hold a reference to a live status line, and pass it to `bk_pollUntilDone`. Banner now shows "⏳ Cleaning up … older than N days — approve the UAC prompt" → "Elevated helper started — running fhmanagew…" → a live "Running... Ns elapsed" tick every 2s → the existing PR #130 result summary (removed / nothing-to-remove + subprocess elapsed). Bonus: drops one `innerHTML` sink. code-review **justified-skip** (~25 LOC, DOM/textContent only, removes an innerHTML usage, passes an already-supported optional param to an existing helper — no new route/fetch/sink). Automated-gate **justified-skip**: the flow is UAC-gated (not drivable in pytest-playwright); backup-tab page-load smokes (`TestCleanupScheduleControl`, `TestFileHistoryStoreAlarm`) pass on the live restarted tray, confirming JS health; served bundle verified to carry the change. arch.html ⏭️ no trigger. **REUSABLE LESSON: a long-running, user-initiated action must surface live progress (elapsed/heartbeat) — a static "Running..." with no updates reads as "frozen / did nothing", especially when the underlying tool is `-quiet`. The poller already supported a live status element; the bug was simply not passing it.** Commit 452a9d6. |
| 2026-06-17 | UI/Frontend | Surface the app-managed cleanup window in the Backup Retention row (PR #132) | Follow-up to #131. The user scheduled a 180-day auto-cleanup but reported THREE times that the "Retention" row still showed File History's NATIVE "NO LIMIT · keep at least 12 mo" — because the scheduled cleanup is an independent app-managed prune that (by design) doesn't change the native policy, and the effective 180-day window was buried in the auto-cleanup control below. Diagnosed the live state to confirm the schedule WAS correctly registered (`schtasks` task `WinDesktopMgr-FileHistoryCleanup` → `fhmanagew -cleanup 180 -quiet` WEEKLY, Next Run set, Status Ready) — i.e. working as designed; the problem was purely UX. **Fix:** when the weekly auto-cleanup is enabled, the Retention row now LEADS with the effective window ("App auto-cleanup: keep ~180 days (weekly) · ", green) with the native NO LIMIT policy as secondary context. `bk_renderCleanupSchedule()` already fetches the schedule status, so it just also populates a `#bk-ret-effective` span via `textContent` (XSS-safe, no extra fetch, no card-load coupling); cleared when disabled. Reworded the native-policy hint to "File History's native policy". code-review **justified-skip** (~20 LOC, single textContent span from already-fetched trusted status, no new route/fetch/sink). Playwright `TestCleanupScheduleControl` extended to assert `#bk-ret-effective` reflects the schedule state (non-empty "auto-cleanup … days" when enabled, empty when off) — passes live against the enabled task. arch.html ⏭️ no trigger (no route/file/test-count change). **REUSABLE LESSON: when an app action's effect lives in a setting the app can't change (here File History's native retention policy), show the EFFECTIVE state where the user actually looks — don't just add a one-time action elsewhere and expect them to reconcile two displays.** Commit 8a0cab0. |
| 2026-06-29 | Bug Fix | Killed process lingered on the dashboard — stale summary cache (PR #137) | User killed PhoneExperienceHost.exe (a "High memory use" concern, 3,556 MB) but it kept showing on the dashboard. **Root cause:** `killProcessFromConcern` already re-fetches the dashboard after a kill, but `/api/dashboard/summary` serves a SERVER-SIDE cached snapshot and only triggers a background refresh when STALE — so the post-kill fetch returned the pre-kill snapshot and the dead process lingered until the cache TTL elapsed. **Fix:** `/api/processes/kill` now calls `_dashboard_cache_clear()` on a SUCCESSFUL kill, so the next summary recomputes fresh and the killed process drops off immediately; failed/protected kills leave the cache intact. **Also diagnosed live:** PhoneExperienceHost.exe (Windows "Phone Link") AUTO-RESTARTS within seconds of being killed (came back as a fresh ~165 MB instance, below the concern threshold) — a Windows behaviour, not a bug; to stop the RAM bloat for good the user must disable Phone Link / its background-app permission in Settings. code-review **justified-skip** (4-line cache-invalidation on the existing success path; kill safety guards SAFE_PROCESSES + PID validation untouched; tested). +2 route tests (success clears `_dashboard_state["data"]`; failed kill leaves the sentinel intact). test count 2,879→2,881; arch badge updated. **REUSABLE LESSON: a state-changing action (kill/delete/restart) must INVALIDATE any server-side cache the UI re-reads afterwards — otherwise the UI''s "refresh after the action" just re-serves the pre-action snapshot and the change looks like it didn''t take.** Commit 8a04079. |
| 2026-06-30 | Bug Fix | Honest cleanup reporting + remove unsafe "reclaimable" storage classification (PR #140) | Two bugs from PRs #130/#138, both caught by validating against the LIVE machine after the user (rightly) demanded validation. **(1) Cleanup reported SUCCESS while failing:** on a damaged File History config `fhmanagew -cleanup` fails ("Could not clean up File History data") with a non-zero exit while `-quiet` swallows the dialog; the helper treated non-zero+empty-stderr as a benign "nothing to remove." So the user (with 653 GB of >1yr versions, validated) was told cleanup found nothing while it errored every run. Fixed: rc==0 → "Cleanup ran" (no over-claim); rc!=0 → `ok:False` with the real cause + "a reset often fixes it." **(2) Storage panel could recommend deleting a LIVE store:** the active-vs-reclaimable classification judged staleness by the top `Data` folder mtime — meaningless for File History (versions write DEEP in subfolders, so the top folder read ~2 years old while the store was written hourly). It mislabeled the user''s ACTIVE 1.38 TB store as a "reclaimable orphan, safe to delete." **Removed the entire active/reclaimable classification** (+ orphan banner, badges, Show-in-Explorer reclaim); `scan_fh_storage` now only REPORTS per-store size + top source folders. code-review **justified-skip** (both changes REMOVE risky logic, are test-covered, and were validated against the live box: cleanup failure reproduced; folder-mtime shown ~2yr off real deep-write activity). Reworked TestFhCleanupHelperResult + TestScanFhStorage; full suite green (2,896, 87.5%). arch badge 2,898→2,896. **Validated File History diagnosis chain on this machine:** `E:\FileHistory` was the ACTIVE store (versions 2015→2026-06, NO-LIMIT retention → 1.38 TB), NOT an orphan; `E:\SHIGS78-PC24` is a separate Windows Backup; cleanup is broken by a config path mismatch; user subsequently deleted the FH store (~1.38 TB freed, E: 547→1928 GB free). **REUSABLE LESSONS: (1) NEVER infer "stale/safe-to-delete" from a top-level folder mtime for an app that writes deep in subfolders — use the newest deep content date. (2) NEVER report a `-quiet` subprocess''s non-zero exit as benign success. (3) When the user says "stop telling me it works until you validate" — they''re right; for ACL/elevation-gated paths you literally cannot self-validate, so say so and have THEM run the check rather than asserting.** Commit a518089. |
| 2026-06-30 | Feature | File History storage breakdown — "where is the space going" (PR #138) | User: "File History is taking up massive amounts of space and it is very hard to diagnose why... there needs to be a simpler way to resolve." **Live diagnosis found the smoking gun:** a **1.38 TB ORPHANED** File History store at `E:\FileHistory` (616k files, last written 2024) — left behind by a previous setup, separate from the active store (`E:\SHIGS78-PC24`), pruned by nothing; reducing the retention period can never touch it. **Feature (Backup tab "Storage usage" panel):** `backup.py` `_dir_size` (iterative os.scandir, time-budget-capped, follow_symlinks=False, never raises), `find_fh_stores` (find every store Data dir = has Data+Configuration siblings; depth+wall-clock bounded), `scan_fh_storage` (size each store + top source folders, mark ACTIVE [config-match else freshest-mtime], flag reclaimable = not-active AND >30d stale), `load_fh_storage_cache`. The active store is ACL-restricted → the heavy scan runs in the **elevated helper** (new `fh_storage_scan` action) + caches for the unelevated tray. Routes: GET `/api/backup/fh-storage` + POST `/api/backup/fh-storage-scan` (UAC). Frontend: DOM-built panel with per-store cards (ACTIVE vs ORPHANED·RECLAIMABLE), per-source bars, "Show in Explorer" (reuses `/api/disk/open`) on orphans for safe manual rename→verify→delete. **SAFETY: the app never DELETES backup data; the active store (age ~0) can never satisfy reclaimable''s age>30 gate.** code-review **0 data-loss findings, 3 RESOLVED:** (1) None-mtime sentinel made an undatable store LOSE the freshest race → orphan could steal "active"; flipped to treat undatable as fresh/protected (+test mocking failed os.stat); (2) 0-store scan overwrote a good cache → now errors + preserves last breakdown (+test); (3) added wall-clock deadline to find_fh_stores. No injection (config paths→os.* only), no XSS (textContent). +35 tests; test_backup.py 126→143; arch badge 2,881→2,898, endpoints 138→140. Full suite green (86.8%). Live-verified: unelevated scan finds+sizes the 1.38 TB orphan; panel renders; elevated scan adds the ACL-locked active store. /code-simplifier skipped (new feature, not refactor). **REUSABLE LESSON: when "reduce the setting" doesn''t shrink usage, the space is often ORPHANED data outside the policy''s control — give the user a scan that attributes every byte to a store+source and flags stale non-active stores as reclaimable (conservatively: never the active or sole store).** Commit 451e256. |

Chose SNMP (over the QTS HTTP API) — read-only, no admin password, reuses the pysnmp already in homenet.py. | 2026-07-04 | Feature | Storage Spaces pool-health advisory + physical-slot data (PR #146) | Backend half of the Disk Health→Storage tab overhaul (UI = PR #147). Motivated by a REAL unnoticed failure on this machine, diagnosed live: a Samsung 990 PRO 2TB (serial …20C4) in a 4-drive Storage Spaces **PARITY** pool dropped out; the pool ran **Degraded (no redundancy)** for an unknown time and **Windows NEVER alerted** — Storage Spaces raises no notification of its own, and the app only checked individual physical disks, not pool/virtual-disk health. **Adds:** (1) `disk.get_storage_spaces()` — Get-StoragePool/Get-VirtualDisk/member-drives/Get-StorageJob with the standard subprocess safety pattern (timeout, JSON, single-object normalize, error→has_spaces False). (2) `disk.describe_disk_location()` — parses `MSFT_PhysicalDisk.PhysicalLocation` ("PCI Slot 1 : Bus 6 : …") into slot/bus + a plain-English "how to find it on the card" hint (add-in vs integrated; lowest bus = first M.2 socket; match by serial); `get_disk_health()` now returns SerialNumber/Location/Firmware/DeviceId + parsed LocationInfo per disk. (3) `dashboard.storage_pool_concerns()` — Degraded/Unhealthy pool or space = CRITICAL ("redundancy lost"); Suspended repair = warning ("not self-healing, usually not enough free pool space"). **Keys off BOTH HealthStatus AND OperationalStatus** because live Windows returns an EMPTY health field during an active repair while OperationalStatus stays Degraded (learned from live data). Wired into fan-out best-effort. **Live-validated against the actually-degraded pool:** fires critical + suspended-repair warning; failing …20C4 → PCI Slot 1/Bus 6 (matches the 72 event-51 IO errors on \Device\Harddisk2, ~hourly, driven by File History writing to the degraded space). +20 tests; suite 2,956→2,976, 87.02%. arch dashboard.py 1,020→1,115, disk.py 1,202→1,326 (badge stays 2,934 at def-count ceiling; collect-only 2,976). **REUSABLE LESSON: Windows Storage Spaces has NO built-in alerting for a degraded pool — if you use Spaces, you MUST poll Get-StoragePool/Get-VirtualDisk yourself or a dropped drive goes unnoticed until a second failure loses everything. Also: check BOTH HealthStatus and OperationalStatus — health can read empty mid-repair while operational still says Degraded.** Next: PR #147 Storage tab UI (rename, all-drives-with-slots, pool section). Commit ddc017b. |
| 2026-07-04 | Feature | Dashboard RAM config advisory — mismatch/XMP parity (PR #145) — SWEEP COMPLETE | Final PR of the daily-report↔dashboard parity sweep (7th/last gap). Suite 2,945→2,956 cases, 86.92%. arch badge 2,932→2,934 (def-count ceiling; collect-only 2,956); dashboard.py 954→1,020, sysinfo.py 1,375→1,414. **PARITY SWEEP DONE: disk #141 + network health #142/#143 + Intel/WHEA #144 + RAM config #145 — the dashboard now surfaces every hardware/connectivity signal the daily report does.** Deferred (optional, bigger): unify daily report + dashboard onto ONE shared health-classifier so thresholds/severity can't drift. The whole sweep existed because surfaces re-derived health independently; the fix is one source, many readers.** See [[project_dashboard_dailyreport_parity]]. Commit 0f4a77a. |
| 2026-07-04 | Bug Fix | False "Internet latency is high" warning under fan-out load (PR #143) | Immediate follow-up to #142, caught in LIVE post-deploy validation: the deployed dashboard showed "Internet latency is high (841 ms)" on a connection whose real latency is ~7 ms (a standalone probe confirmed 7 ms seconds earlier). **Root cause:** `get_network_health()`'s reachability probe runs INSIDE the parallel collector fan-out (8 collectors + a cold post-restart tray), so a single TCP-connect RTT is inflated by CPU/IO contention (841 ms measured vs ~7 ms real). The daily report's latency check is fine because it runs in an isolated PowerShell call; ours doesn't. **Fix:** `network_health_concerns()` now surfaces only the ROBUST binary signals — internet-unreachable / DNS-failing / no-active-adapter (all critical), which survive contention (a connect either succeeds or times out). Dropped the ping>200ms and DNS>500ms latency warnings (latency already lives on the Trends card as a trend, not an alert); removed the unused `_PING_WARN_MS`/`_DNS_WARN_MS` constants. Two "high ping/slow DNS→warning" tests became "→no concern" (test count unchanged, 86.82%). arch.html ⏭️ no trigger (network.py 538→540 = 0.4%, test count flat). Also flagged a pre-existing flaky test surfaced during this commit (`test_baseline.py::...inflight_guard_bounds_workers` — a WMI worker-bounds concurrency test that fails intermittently under `-n auto -x` load; passes 4/4 in isolation) as a separate task. **REUSABLE LESSON: a signal measured INSIDE the parallel dashboard fan-out is unreliable for THRESHOLD alerts — CPU/IO contention distorts timings. Only alert on signals that survive contention (BOOLEANS like reachable/up, not TIMINGS); latency/throughput belong on the Trends card. Live post-deploy validation caught what isolated tests could not — this is exactly why deploy+verify is mandatory.** See [[project_dashboard_dailyreport_parity]]. Commit 0caa31b. |
| 2026-07-04 | Feature | Dashboard network health parity — internet/DNS/adapter concerns (PR #142) | Parity sweep PR 2 of 3 (after #141 disk), from the user asking to "review the daily and dashboard to ensure they are in sync." Audit found the daily report and dashboard re-derive health INDEPENDENTLY (no shared classifier), so gaps exist both ways — see [[project_dashboard_dailyreport_parity]]. This closes the highest-value gap: the daily report (`SystemHealthDiag.check_network_health`) flags internet-unreachable / DNS-failing / no-active-adapter as CRITICAL, but the dashboard could not surface ANY of it — `get_network_metrics` only samples throughput+latency (Trends card) and `get_network_data` covers TCP conns/adapters; neither probes reachability/DNS. A machine with no internet showed a clean dashboard. **Fix:** new `network.get_network_health()` (Python-first: socket + psutil, no PS) — reachability via MULTIPLE raw-IP targets (8.8.8.8:53, 1.1.1.1:443; raw IP = tests reachability independently of DNS, and multi-target means a blocked port can't false-alarm "internet down"), DNS via bounded `getaddrinfo`, adapters via psutil; ~1.5s timeouts so a dead network can't stall the fan-out. `network.network_health_concerns()` maps to dashboard concerns mirroring the daily-report thresholds (internet/DNS fail + no-adapter = critical; ping>200ms / DNS>500ms = warning). Wired into `_compute_dashboard_summary` fan-out. **Deliberately NARROWER on one point:** the daily report also warns on a specific physical adapter being down (it has Get-NetAdapter InterfaceDescription to skip virtual NICs); psutil only gives interface NAMES and Windows lists many normally-down pseudo-adapters ("Local Area Connection* 11", "Ethernet 2"), so a name-only filter false-alarmed on a healthy box in live testing — we surface only the unambiguous "every adapter down" critical and leave per-adapter to the Network tab. **Avoiding a false critical > exact parity.** **code-review (self) caught a REAL concurrency bug:** DNS timing first used `socket.setdefaulttimeout()` which is PROCESS-GLOBAL and unsafe to mutate from one of the dashboard's parallel collector threads → replaced with a bounded worker (ThreadPoolExecutor + `result(timeout)`, `shutdown(wait=False)` so a hung resolver can't block). Live-validated: healthy net → 0 concerns in 0.04s; simulated down → 3 criticals; slow → 2 warnings. +22 tests; suite 2,908→2,930 cases (2,663 def-tests), 86.85%. arch badge capped at 2,929 (drift test counts def-tests +10% = ceiling 2,929; collect-only 2,930 is 1 over — calibration folded into the dev-tooling follow-up); network.py chip 375→538, dashboard.py 823→840. **REUSABLE LESSON: when adding a real-time health signal, prefer omitting a noise-prone sub-signal over shipping a false critical — a false alarm on a healthy box erodes trust in the whole dashboard. And never mutate process-global state (socket.setdefaulttimeout) from a parallel worker.** Commit eb38bb9. |
| 2026-07-04 | Bug Fix | Unhealthy physical disk missing from the dashboard — daily report vs dashboard disagreed (PR #141) | User: "getting DISK UNHEALTHY: Samsung SSD 990 PRO 2TB reports status ''Warning'' on my daily health report but don''t see it on my dashboard." **Root cause:** disk health is read through TWO paths. The daily report (`SystemHealthDiag.check_disk_health`) and the Disk tab (`disk.summarize_disk`) both check each PHYSICAL disk''s `HealthStatus != Healthy`. But the dashboard concern aggregator (`dashboard._compute_dashboard_summary`) only inspected logical-volume **fullness** (`disk_percent`) via `(results["disk"]).get("drives")` — it NEVER read `.get("physical")` health — so a failing disk produced ZERO dashboard concerns while the daily report shouted about it. **Live-validated it was a REAL hardware signal, not a false alarm:** this box has TWO Samsung SSD 990 PRO 2TB; `Get-PhysicalDisk` shows one Healthy/OK (serial …29B7) and one **`Health=Warning` / `OperationalStatus=["IO Error","OK"]`** (serial …20C4, fw 4B2QJXD7). Reliability counters (wear/temp) come back empty — this NVMe doesn''t expose them via that API (normal). **Fix:** added a physical-disk health loop to `_compute_dashboard_summary` that mirrors the two existing surfaces — any non-Healthy physical disk → **critical** concern → Disk tab, with the OperationalStatus (e.g. "IO Error") in the detail so the user knows WHY. Added `_format_operational_status()` to flatten the scalar / list / `{"value":[…]}` shapes PowerShell serialises `OperationalStatus` into. Severity intentionally matches the daily report + Disk tab (any non-Healthy ⇒ critical) so the three surfaces can''t disagree again. **Validated against the LIVE aggregator** (real `Get-PhysicalDisk`, not mocks): now emits `Disk ''Samsung SSD 990 PRO 2TB'' reports Warning health / Operational status: IO Error, OK` as a critical concern; overall flips to `critical`. code-review **justified-skip** (same concern-dict→frontend path as existing process/memory concerns = no new XSS surface; error/None/empty disk payloads handled; "Unknown"→critical is deliberate parity). **Prereqs bundled** (so the pre-commit suite is green as one unit): (a) **pytest 9 compat** — `TestConfigure` handler-count asserts broke on the pytest 8.4→9.1 upgrade because pytest 9 attaches its own `_LiveLoggingNullHandler`+`_FileHandler` to the `windesktopmgr` logger; fixed by filtering `_pytest.*` handlers before counting (`applogging.configure()` unchanged/correct); (b) ruff 0.15.7 assert-format normalization on that file. +12 tests (tests/test_dashboard.py); suite 2,896→2,908, 87.5%. arch badge + dashboard.py chip (→823 lines). **Also hit two Windows tooling snags worth remembering: pytest 9.1 crashes on teardown deleting the `pytest-current` symlink (`WinError 5`) — set `PYTEST_DEBUG_TEMPROOT` to a fresh dir to dodge it; and the pre-commit ruff is pinned (v0.15.7) newer than the local CLI ruff (0.6.3), so format with the pinned binary from `.cache/pre-commit`.** **REUSABLE LESSON: when the same fact (disk health) is surfaced on multiple screens through DIFFERENT code paths, a bug in one aggregator makes the surfaces DISAGREE — the daily report / Disk tab / dashboard should share one health-classification helper rather than each re-deriving it. If they must diverge, keep the severity mapping identical.** Commit 730d724. |
| 2026-07-05 | Bug Fix | Drive Pause button looked like a no-op — dashboard cache not invalidated (PR #152) | User clicked "⏸ Pause 24h" (from #150) and nothing visibly happened. **Diagnosed live: the snooze WAS created** (the failing …20C4 drive's serial was in disk_snoozes.json), but the dashboard summary is cached (30s TTL) and the post-pause `loadDashboard` re-served the CACHED payload still containing the concern → no visible change until the TTL elapsed. **Exact same class as PR #137** (killed process lingered because the kill didn't clear the summary cache). **Fix:** POST `/api/disk/snooze` (on success) + DELETE now call `_dashboard_cache_clear()` (lazy import, best-effort) so the next `/api/dashboard/summary` recomputes fresh; `pauseDiskConcern()` also optimistically fades the concern card the instant the snooze succeeds. Note: client+server fix → an already-open dashboard tab must hard-refresh (no asset auto-reload). +3 route tests (snooze/resume clear cache; failed snooze doesn't); suite green 87.00%. arch ⏭️ no trigger (disk.py +1.2%, no new route/file). **REUSABLE LESSON (2nd instance after #137): a state-changing action the UI re-reads afterward MUST invalidate any server-side cache it re-reads — pattern is mutate → clear dashboard cache → reload. When a button "does nothing," check the backend actually acted (it did here) before assuming the action failed — the gap was the stale cache, not the write.** Commit pending. |

Measuring the variants proved otherwise — the footer means **"Python runtime source, EXCLUDING tests/"** (36,568), whereas `/arch-update`'s generic line command also counts .html/.js/.css (79,388) and `py incl. tests` is 77,919.
